# cuttle: agent-experience issues and improvements

Research artifact, 2026-08-18. Covers cuttle at `6b2a4bf` (v0.11.4).

Two independent investigations, run as multi-agent workflows:

1. **Session audit** - every cuttle browser session from a 14-day window read in full,
   mined for what actually went wrong for agents driving the browser.
2. **Upstream sweep** - six related projects analysed for capabilities and
   community-discovered approaches cuttle is missing.

The two were run blind to each other and only merged at the end. Where they agree, the
finding is strong. Where they disagree, the disagreement is recorded rather than smoothed
over - section 6 lists what was proposed and rejected, and why.

**Everything in this document is anonymized.** Session identifiers are stable opaque labels
(`S01`..`S48`); the label-to-identifier mapping is deliberately not in this repo. Site names,
account identifiers, personal paths and any value that could identify a person or an account
have been replaced with category descriptions. Upstream references (repos, commits, PR
numbers) are public and are cited exactly.

**Layout.** This directory holds the whole artifact: `README.md` is the analysis and every
appendix; `data/` holds the untruncated anonymized source behind it - `session-audit.json`
(ranked and clustered issues), `upstream-analysis.json` (recommendations and theme gaps),
`upstream-repos.json` (per-repo community items) and `upstream-deltas.json` (per-repo code
deltas, critics and cross-session notes).

## 1. Method and corpus

### 1.1 Session audit

64 sessions from 2026-08-04 to 2026-08-18 (46 root sessions, 18 subagent sessions), selected
by SQL over the session store: any session with at least one tool call invoking `cuttle`,
`playwright-cli`, `agent-browser` or `browser-use`. Each transcript was read end to end,
paging until exhausted, by an Opus agent; the six heaviest sessions got a second independent
read with a different lens (subtle inefficiency rather than loud failure). Raw findings were
then clustered, attacked by two adversarial critics, and ranked.

- 22 agents, 4.35M tokens, 392 tool calls, 47 min wall clock, 0 errors.
- 39 of the 64 read sessions had actually driven pages through cuttle.
- 353 raw findings from the first pass, 394 recoverable including the second pass.
- 63 distinct clustered issues, 47 ranked.

**Volume, measured over the window rather than estimated:** 2,749 driver invocations, 1,337
`cuttle` CLI invocations, 968 `eval`/`run-code` calls, 592 snapshot calls, 661 browser calls
carrying a literal `sleep`, 185 synthetic-click calls across 21 sessions, 113 driver
screenshots total. cuttle's own verb usage: `up` 37, `downloads` 44, `status` 25, `--context`
13, `skill` 5, `open` 2, `context` 1.

**A known limitation of the selection.** Sessions were scoped by *session creation date*
inside the window. The synthesis agent independently re-queried by message activity and found
**114** sessions issuing a browser command (50 root + 64 subagent) and **72** that actually
drove pages - more than the 64 selected, because a session created before 2026-08-04 but
active inside it was missed. The read set therefore covers the highest-volume sessions but is
not the complete population. Frequency counts below are lower bounds.

**A correction the synthesis made to its own inputs.** The clustering pass counted
`sessions_affected` in a way that mixed root and subagent sessions, so one investigation
(a root plus its four subagent audits) inflated to five. The ranked list corrects this; the
raw counts in Appendix A do not.

### 1.2 Upstream sweep

Six repositories, freshly pulled. Local clones were 1 to 3 months stale, so the delta below is
what we had not seen:

| Repo | Was at | Now at | Stale by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `microsoft/playwright-cli` | `793cfb3` (2026-07-09, v0.1.17) | `4f9d85a` (2026-08-13) | 5 weeks |
| `vercel-labs/agent-browser` | `afae698` (2026-07-08) | `548b159` (2026-08-10, v0.34.0) | 5 weeks |
| `CloakHQ/CloakBrowser` | `2442c32` (2026-08-08) | `a91d105` (2026-08-17) | 9 days |
| `CloakHQ/CloakBrowser-Manager` | `a85b213` (2026-05-26, v0.0.10) | `9dd49cf` (2026-08-18) | **12 weeks** |
| `clark-labs-inc/clark-browser` | `df1afb6` (2026-06-22) | unchanged on main; releases moved | - |
| `clearcotelabs/clearcote-browser` | - | `da07119` (2026-08-16, 0.27.0) | - |

Each repo got a code-delta agent (git log, diffs, agent-facing docs, full verb surface) and a
community agent (issues, PRs, discussions, maintainer replies). Ten cross-repo theme sweeps
ran concurrently. Two adversarial critics then attacked the result: one for cargo-culted or
stealth-breaking recommendations, one for coverage gaps.

- 25 agents, 3.62M tokens, 1,125 tool calls, 39 min wall clock, 0 errors.
- 362 code-delta and community items, 92 distinct capability gaps, 26 ranked recommendations.

**"cuttle-browser" is not a public repository.** `gh search repos` returns nothing. It was
read as the stealth-Chromium engines cuttle actually builds on, so `clark-browser` and
`clearcote-browser` were swept in its place.

---

## 2. The four reported issues, traced to source

These were reported from daily use before either workflow ran. All four are real. Two are
narrower than they appear, and two are worse.

### 2.1 The browser window does not fill the VNC viewer

**Confirmed, deliberate, and fixable without losing what it protects.**

The framebuffer is hard-coded: `ops/docker/bin/docker-entrypoint.sh` starts
`Xvnc :99 -geometry 1920x1080`. Each seed's Chrome is sized to *its own spoofed screen*:
`fingerprint.ScreenArgs()` picks a per-seed resolution from a table (Windows persona:
1920x1080, 1536x864, 1366x768, 1440x900; macOS persona: 1440x900 through 1728x1117) and emits
`--window-size=<w>,<h-taskbar>`. `dropMaximizeIfSized()` (`internal/serve/pool.go:466`) then
strips the entrypoint's `--start-maximized` whenever a `--window-size` is pinned. Its own
comment states the tradeoff plainly: the flag exists "purely so a human viewer gets a
full-looking window, which is worth less than a coherent identity."

The coherence it protects is real. The binary spoofs `screen.*` and `window.outer*` from the
seed, but `window.inner*` is the actual window. Unpin the size and a seed reports `outerWidth`
1536 around an `innerWidth` of 780 - a window with 750px of invisible chrome, which no real
browser has.

So a seed drawing 1366x768 genuinely occupies about two thirds of a 1920x1080 framebuffer.
The fix is not to resize the window; it is to **size the framebuffer to the seed** (recommendation
13). There is a second, larger consequence recorded separately: the driver's default 1920
viewport exceeds the seed's spoofed `screen.width` in roughly 75% of renders, which is a
detectable contradiction independent of how the viewer looks.

### 2.2 Agents fumble secrets and ordinary typing

**Confirmed, with a mechanical root cause that makes it a live bug, not a rough edge.**

`insertTextMaxRunes = 80` (`internal/serve/humanize.go:156`). At the humanizer's own measured
pacing - `interKeyDelay` around 103ms plus `keyHold` around 25ms plus the typo roll, so roughly
133ms per character - 80 runes is a **10.6 second** budget. playwright-cli's `--timeout-action`
default is **5000ms** (`README.md:558`).

Every value between roughly 38 and 80 characters times out the driver *today*. That is exactly
the API-key and long-password band. Worse, the failure is not clean: `h.sleep` aborts only on
connection teardown, so cuttle keeps typing after the driver has given up, the agent retries
`fill`, and the value is typed twice into a field that already contains it.

Above the cap the command is forwarded verbatim (the silent bypass at `humanize.go:592`), so
the highest-stakes values arrive as a single IME-style commit with zero `keydown`/`keyup` -
precisely the `fill()` tell the rewrite exists to remove, applied to exactly the wrong inputs.

cuttle also owns no secret path of its own. It defers entirely to playwright-cli's
attach-time `PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_SECRETS_FILE` substitution, which is driver-side, all-or-nothing,
and types unknown names literally into the page when the file is missing an entry.

### 2.3 Downloads and screenshots feel like a hassle

**Split verdict. Downloads work and are used. Screenshots have no path at all.**

Downloads are the healthiest capability in the product: pinned into the seed profile dir,
served over `GET /downloads` and `/downloads/{name}`, pulled with `cuttle downloads`, 0600 on
disk, content never printed. 44 invocations in the window - the second most-used verb.

Screenshots have no cuttle-side path whatsoever. There is no verb, no endpoint, and a driver's
screenshot lands on the *driver host's* cwd, which SKILL.md itself flags as a trap. The
measured consequence: **55 of the 72 page-driving sessions (76%) took zero screenshots**, while
one session took 22 that its own audit calls pure waste. Six sessions hit hard capture
failures, including three identical `TimeoutError: ... waiting for fonts to load` on three
unrelated sites, after which that session abandoned visual verification entirely.

There is a deeper structural point: `Page.captureScreenshot` only captures the renderer
surface. The JS dialog, the permission bubble, the download shelf and the window manager state
are **invisible to every screenshot any driver can take** - which is exactly why the reported
screenshot showed a dialog stacked over a modal that the agent could not see.

### 2.4 Agents are blind to dialogs, alerts and overlays

**Confirmed and the most damaging class, but the fix is narrower than "build dialog handling".**

playwright-cli already solves most of it: per-tab `ModalState[]`, every snapshot and action
wrapped in a `Promise.race` against modal-state events, `defineTabTool` refusing any tool
without `clearsModalState`, and `dialog-accept` / `dialog-dismiss` surfaced in its own skill
file. agent-browser goes further with `start_dialog_handler` and a
`dispatch_mouse_or_dialog` that races the input ack against `javascriptDialogOpening`.

cuttle's `internal/cli/SKILL.md` contains **zero** occurrences of dialog, alert, modal, popup
or untrusted. So this is first a routing gap: agents are not told the verbs exist. In an
892-call session there were 4 `dialog-accept` and 1 `dialog-dismiss` calls total; every other
dialog was discovered by inference after commands mysteriously stopped working.

The genuine capability gap is narrower and no driver can close it. Chromium's
`PageHandler::Disable()` (`content/browser/devtools/protocol/page_handler.cc:690-714`)
deliberately **leaves a pending dialog hanging** when the WebContents has a real dialog manager
- which headed Chrome always does - with no replay on a later `Page.enable`. Chrome outlives
every driver in cuttle's model, so a dialog raised between attachments, or left pending when a
driver disconnects, wedges that tab permanently: `Page.handleJavaScriptDialog` from any future
driver returns "No dialog is showing". Only the always-attached daemon can cover that window.

Observed cost: four separate "Leave site?" episodes on one completed tax-form session, one of
which destroyed the draft ("The navigation had already fired, so the in-browser draft is
gone"), and a cross-driver wedge that took a `dialog-dismiss` plus a session close to clear.

---

## 3. Ranked issues: what costs agents the most

47 ranked issues from the session corpus, by impact times frequency. Full evidence for each is
in Appendix A; the twelve that dominate cost are expanded here.

### The critical set

**1. No write returns a post-condition.** *(critical, daemon, 16 sessions)*
Clicks, fills and saves report success while changing nothing, so every session hand-builds a
bespoke verification loop after every mutation. The largest single call-cost class in the
corpus: one session spent roughly 400 of 892 calls (46%) answering "what is actually on the
page". One built an out-of-band protocol - save, `cuttle downloads`, grep the form XML - after
*every single field*, downloading the same file nine times and tracking Chrome's `(3).xml` to
`(9).xml` dedup counter by hand. Corpus-wide there are 592 snapshot calls against 2,749 driver
calls: roughly one page re-read per four actions, almost all of it read-back.
**Gap:** every cuttle-owned write returns the post-condition instead of a checkmark - click
landed at (x,y) on `<el>`, DOM subtree changed or not, active element after, field value before
and after. The daemon already computes most of this in the humanized-click verify and discards it.

**2. Input to a non-foreground tab is silently swallowed.** *(critical, daemon, 7 sessions)*
Background tabs get near-zero `requestAnimationFrame`, so actionability never settles and lazy
loaders never fire. One root cause that wore four different disguises over two weeks. Measured
on live containers: `noDefaults:true` background gives fps 0 and `click FAIL 15002ms`;
`noDefaults:false` gives fps 61 and `click OK 851ms`. One session spent 58 calls concluding
"cuttle's Chromium never fires the lazy RSC requests" and handed the task back to the user,
then found it twenty minutes later: "Found it - `document.hidden === true`. That was the whole
bug - a background tab, not a cuttle limitation." 139 timeout-signature results, and 0 of 8
identical retries ever worked.
**Gap:** extend the focus pin to page-visibility, pass the renderer anti-backgrounding launch
flags, expose per-target foreground state in `cuttle status`, and error when Input is
dispatched at a target that cannot receive it rather than dropping it silently.

**3. No trusted-click escape hatch, so agents reach for `isTrusted:false`.** *(critical, daemon, 21 sessions)*
When the driver's click fails, agents escalate to force-click, DOM `dispatchEvent` and
hand-computed coordinates - throwing away the product's only reason to exist. **185 synthetic
clicks across 21 sessions**, verified by SQL. One agent caught itself doing it: "Making
`dispatchEvent` my reflex quietly throws away the stealth property on exactly the anti-bot sites
where we need it." Another rebuilt clicking from scratch - 27 evals whose only job was
`getBoundingClientRect`, each followed by manual `mousemove; mousedown; mouseup`, with
coordinates stale on any reflow.
**Gap:** `cuttle click <selector|x,y>` that resolves, scrolls into view, foregrounds the target
and dispatches humanized real Input in one call - plus a hard SKILL.md rule forbidding
`dispatchEvent`, paired with that alternative so the rule is followable.

**4. The a11y snapshot omits exactly the controls real apps use.** *(critical, mixed, 10 sessions)*
Save buttons, OAuth links, expanders, Angular/LWC/WebForms and shadow-DOM widgets. This is the
direct upstream cause of issue 3. One session: `snapshot | rg -i 'button.*save|publish'`
returned only a date string, while a raw eval over `document.querySelectorAll('button')` found
the Save control immediately. Another had roughly 20 of 35 calls as raw evals because the a11y
layer was useless on that stack.
**Gap:** `cuttle extract --selector ... --format json` returning form values keyed by nearest
stable id/name/label, piercing shadow DOM and spanning frames.

**5. Multiple clients share one browser with no ownership.** *(critical, daemon, 7 sessions)*
A human or a second agent silently clobbers the agent's work, and the surfaced error always
blames the selector. Two sessions ran against the same container simultaneously and were each
other's invisible saboteur; one stopped and handed the task back ("we're both driving the same
browser and it's clobbering the work - that also explains the three clicks that did nothing"),
the other had already corrupted a live config. A deployment-scale version exists too: local dev
and production compute the same deterministic seed and land on the same single-replica Chrome,
where "either side's `browser.close()` can kill the other's page".
**Gap:** per-attach target ownership or an advisory lease, attached-client count and foreground
owner in `cuttle status`, and a briefing line telling an agent to claim a context.

**6. Discoverability, not capability, is the binding constraint.** *(critical, skill-docs, corpus-wide)*
Shipped affordances that exactly solve top-ranked problems went essentially unused. `--context`
is the precise fix for the issue-5 collision, is never mentioned in the `cuttle up` briefing,
and was reached for **once in 14 days**. One session read the driver's own docs describing
`run-code` and then made zero `run-code` calls in 183 browser calls; its own audit put roughly
65 of those as collapsible to 5. Another truncated the briefing to `tail -2` to save context,
discarding the routing rules entirely. A third opened a non-cuttle browser for a second identity
until the user asked "are you using cuttle? or some magic stuff yourself?" - it did not know
`cuttle up --name` existed.
**Gap:** a short, stable briefing - short enough that nobody truncates it - naming existing
escape hatches against the *symptoms* that trigger them, with the full guide behind `cuttle skill`.

**7. Operational facts live nowhere durable, and SKILL.md carries false claims.**
*(critical, skill-docs, 11 sessions)*
The same four driver mechanics were independently re-derived in at least five sessions.
Three different user repos now carry hand-rolled cuttle documentation, because the knowledge had
nowhere else to go. On the accuracy half: SKILL.md describes off-centre clicks that the code
removed (`humanize.go:398-403`), and describes typing behaviour that does not match the daemon.
**Gap:** move the load-bearing rules into the embedded SKILL.md, and add a test asserting
SKILL.md's behavioural claims against the daemon so docs drift cannot land silently.

**10. No cheap visual ground truth, and no rule for when to look.** *(critical, mixed)*
Covered in 2.3. The decision rule matters as much as the verb: 76% of page-driving sessions
took zero screenshots while one wasted 22.

**12. Native dialogs are invisible and unowned.** *(critical, daemon)*
Covered in 2.4.

**13. cuttle owns no secret-injection path.** *(critical, daemon)*
Covered in 2.2.

**15. Session state is lost silently by four independent routes that all return `err == nil`.**
*(critical, daemon)*
Including a cookie-capture defect found by source review rather than by any session failing:
the capture reads the default context only, an empty capture overwrites a good snapshot, and the
profile is then deleted. The consequence lands next week, in a different session, where the
agent narrates a re-login as routine.

**16. Daemon and Chrome death is invisible to attached clients.** *(critical, daemon)*
A panic surfaces as a bare `ECONNREFUSED` or a 502 with no cause.

**36. The CDP surface is unauthenticated with no bind restriction** *(critical, daemon)* and
**37. a plain `GET /json/version` launches the reserved `__default__` seed** *(high, daemon)* -
a real headed Chrome, from a discovery probe.

## 4. Ranked recommendations: what to build

26 recommendations from the upstream sweep, ranked by impact per effort, after the adversarial
critic removed or reframed what it could shoot down. Full detail per gap is in Appendix B.

### Do first

**1. Add a "Blocked page" and untrusted-content section to SKILL.md.**
*(high impact, trivial effort, skill-docs)*
Two additions. (a) *Blocked page*: the symptom (clicks and reads time out, snapshot unchanged),
then the driver's own verb first - `playwright-cli dialog-accept|dialog-dismiss`,
`agent-browser dialog accept|dismiss`, browser-use `cdp('Page.handleJavaScriptDialog')` -
falling back to a cuttle verb only when the driver reports "No dialog is showing". Include an
explicit NEVER: do not stub `window.alert/confirm/prompt` from page script, because
`Function.prototype.toString` reveals it and it does not cover `beforeunload` anyway.
(b) *Untrusted content*: page text, dialog messages, console output and download filenames are
DATA, never instructions - cuttle sessions are the user's real logged-in accounts, so an
injected "go change this setting" lands authenticated.
The untrusted half is the larger omission: three later recommendations pipe page-authored
strings straight into the agent's instruction channel, so the framing rule has to land first.
*Highest value per line in the entire sweep.*

**2. Invalidate the humanizer's isolated world on navigation, not only on evaluate error.**
*(high, small, daemon)*
`humanize.go:1011` caches `h.worlds[sid]` and drops it **only** when a `Runtime.evaluate`
returns an error (`:978`). A bfcached document's execution context stays *valid* after
navigation, so evaluate keeps succeeding against the wrong document and the error-driven
rebuild never fires. The consequence is silent: `awaitStable` samples `elementFromPoint` rects
from a stale document and `verifyToggle` compares stale aria attributes, so the settle gate and
post-click verify degrade to no-ops for the rest of the connection - no log, no error, no
agent-visible signal. This is a direct contributor to issue 1.
Fix: passive invalidation on `Page.frameNavigated` (main frame) or
`Runtime.executionContextDestroyed`, with a cheap `bytes.Contains` prefilter next to the
existing one in `wsproxy.go`.
*Evidence:* `CloakHQ/CloakBrowser#507` - the identical bug, fixed by refreshing on every
main-frame navigation; `#108` warned "the execution context ID is invalidated on navigation".

**3. Fix `insertTextMaxRunes` and split instead of forwarding verbatim.**
*(critical, small, daemon)*
Three changes: lower the cap from 80 to roughly 30 so the typing budget fits inside the driver's
5000ms default; above the cap type the first N runes as real keystrokes and commit the remainder
via one `Input.insertText`, so the keystroke record is never empty; and make abandonment safe -
if the client sends a new command on the same session or a wall-clock budget expires, stop and
answer with a CDP error naming how many runes landed. Update `SKILL.md:214-218`, which currently
documents the 80-rune cliff as fact.
Explicitly *rejects* the alternative of raising `PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_TIMEOUT_ACTION` to 30000: a 6x
timeout makes every blocked-page failure take 30 seconds to surface, working directly against
recommendations 1 and 4.

**4. Daemon dialog observer, scoped to the window no driver can cover.**
*(high, medium, mixed)*
New `internal/serve/dialogs.go`: one long-lived daemon CDP connection per seed doing
`Target.setAutoAttach{flatten:true}` plus `Page.enable` per page session, tracking dialog state
on `chromeInstance`. `GET /dialogs`, `POST /dialogs/{seed}/handle`, `cuttle dialogs` and
`cuttle dialog accept|dismiss`, one line in `cuttle status`.
The scoping is the whole design: **answer a dialog only when `p.conns[seedKey] == 0`**. Observe
only while a driver is attached, so the driver's own handler always wins. Everything an attached
driver already handles stays out of scope. Also gate the humanizer - when a dialog is pending,
skip `awaitStable` and `verifyToggle` entirely rather than burning 2s each against a paused
renderer.

**5. Desktop framebuffer capture, cropped to the seed's window.** *(high, small, mixed)*
`Page.captureScreenshot` cannot see browser chrome, so the dialog, permission bubble and
download shelf are structurally invisible to every driver screenshot. KasmVNC is already
installed and already serving on the same port; its developer API exposes `/api/get_screenshot`.
**The crop is not optional:** an uncropped grab of the shared `:99` returns every live seed's
window in one image, over the same loopback port that serves `/downloads` - a cross-identity
leak.
This deliberately drops the parallel CDP-screenshot endpoint the sweep also proposed: that is
playwright-cli's `screenshot` rebuilt one layer down, and it cannot see the thing that motivated
the request.

### The two stealth defects

**6. Resolve the WebRTC no-op: add the policy flags, delete or implement the dead switch.**
*(critical, trivial, mixed)*
`--fingerprint-webrtc-ip` is declared in `clark_fingerprint_switches.cc` and whitelisted into
the renderer by patch 0050, and is **read by no patch in the series**. clark's own
`PATCHES.md:124` marks that patch status `"spec"` - never implemented. cuttle pays a live network
round-trip through the proxy to resolve an exit IP, passes a flag the binary ignores, and ships a
documented guarantee it does not deliver. Meanwhile nothing suppresses non-proxied UDP, so ICE
enumerates the container's real interfaces and STUN produces a srflx candidate from the real
egress path: a proxied seed's WebRTC contradicts its own geoip timezone and locale.
clark's shipped mitigation is a WebRTC policy argument set (RFC 8828 Mode 4) promoted to a
proxy-triggered default. clearcote `#13` shows the harder half - TURN Allocate over UDP bypasses
the gathering-time check entirely, and fabricated srflx candidates with zero host candidates are
self-contradicting.

**7. Patch blink `MediaValues` so CSS media queries stop contradicting the JS spoof.**
*(critical, small, engine-patches)*
No `media_values` patch exists anywhere in the 24-patch series. Patch 0011 spoofs `screen.*` and
0013 hard-returns `devicePixelRatio` 2.0 for the macOS persona, but `MediaValues` still reads
`display::ScreenInfo` and `frame->DevicePixelRatio()`. So on every arm64 seed
`window.devicePixelRatio === 2` while `matchMedia('(resolution: 2dppx)')` and
`(-webkit-device-pixel-ratio: 2)` are **both false**, and `matchMedia('(min-device-width: 1920px)')`
is true for a seed reporting 1366. Two single-expression detections that need no reference corpus.
clearcote already ships the fix: `patches/141-media-queries.patch`, persona-keying
`CalculateDeviceWidth/Height/DevicePixelRatio/PointerTypes/HoverTypes/ColorGamut`. Worth folding
in `CloakBrowser-Manager#8` at the same time: KasmVNC's virtual pointer has no X `device_type`, so
`(pointer: fine)` and `(hover: hover)` return false and mainstream detectors flag tampering. Same
file family, one patch.

### The rest

| # | Impact | Effort | Layer | Recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 8 | high | medium | mixed | Daemon-side secret sentinel typed by the humanizer |
| 9 | high | small | daemon | Report what the click actually landed on |
| 10 | high | small | daemon | Block viewport and window-bounds overrides at the proxy |
| 11 | high | trivial | daemon | Block `Browser.close` so a driver cannot take the seed down |
| 12 | high | medium | daemon | Per-frame isolated worlds so iframe clicks are actually gated |
| 13 | high | medium | image-viewer | Size the framebuffer to the seed before launch; raise the right window for handoff |
| 14 | high | small | daemon | Verify the typed value landed, without leaking it |
| 15 | medium | small | cli | Machine-readable status, identity headers, and a doctor verb |
| 16 | high | medium | mixed | Push files into the container; stop silent upload failures |
| 17 | high | medium | daemon | Turn download events on; stop drivers redirecting the pin |
| 18 | high | medium | daemon | Replace the single-Bezier mouse with sum-of-minimum-jerk submovements, plus a scored regression test |
| 19 | medium | small | daemon | Derive the motor persona from the fingerprint seed |
| 20 | high | medium | driver | Add an adversarial detection lane |
| 21 | medium | small | engine-patches | Cherry-pick clark's AnalyserNode noise patch (0027) |
| 22 | medium | medium | mixed | Give every failure a code and the data to recover from it |
| 23 | medium | medium | daemon | Per-seed humanize speed tiers, settable without a restart |
| 24 | medium | medium | image-viewer | Clipboard relay for the human-handoff leg |
| 25 | medium | trivial | daemon | Suppress typo injection on secrets and segmented fields |
| 26 | low | trivial | daemon | Seed the cursor somewhere plausible; log unhandled Input methods |

**On recommendation 9** - the cheapest of the lot. cuttle already hit-tests the exact click point
in `awaitStable` and then says nothing about it. A cookie wall or app modal that animates in
during the gate's own 40-590ms window plus the pre-press dwell is invisible: the driver reports
success, the agent re-snapshots, sees nothing changed, and re-clicks. agent-browser's
`BLOCKER_AT_JS` names the covering element instead ("is covered by `<div#consent-banner>` at its
click point - dismiss or interact with the covering element first"). The machinery already
exists in cuttle; only the reporting is missing.

**On recommendation 18** - clearcote is the only project in the set that *measures* its motion
model. Its `motionscore.ts` detector emulator scored a faithful port of cuttle's `planMouseMove`
at **0.645** against their **0.953**, with `velocity-symmetric` firing on **187 of 200 runs**.
cuttle is literally the `oldGlide()` baseline they wrote the new model to beat.

---

## 5. Three patterns underneath everything

Read together, the 47 issues and 26 recommendations are mostly three problems wearing different
clothes.

### 5.1 Nothing in the stack reports a post-condition

Issues 1, 2, 3, 9, 12, 15, 16, 19, 22, 23, 30 and 39 are all the same defect at different layers:
an operation returns "ok" and the agent has no way to learn what actually happened. The daemon
frequently *knows* - `awaitStable` hit-tests the click point, `verifyToggle` polls the aria state,
the pool observes Chrome exits, the capture path counts cookies - and discards all of it.

This is why the corpus shows one page re-read per four actions. The agent is reconstructing, at
great cost and unreliably, state the daemon already computed and threw away. Recommendations 9,
14 and 22 are the cheap end of fixing it; issue 1's gap is the full statement.

### 5.2 The agent cannot see the parts of the browser that block it

Dialogs, permission bubbles, the download shelf, the window manager, another client's cursor,
the framebuffer itself. Every one of these lives *outside* the renderer surface, which is the
only thing CDP exposes and therefore the only thing any driver can screenshot or snapshot.

This is a structural argument for a cuttle-side capability rather than better driver usage: the
blind spot is exactly the region only the container host can observe. Recommendations 4, 5 and
13 all sit in it.

### 5.3 The stealth guarantee is asserted, never measured

cuttle has a golden-file tripwire on *arguments*, and nothing on *behaviour*. The consequences
found here: a flag that is a documented no-op (rec 6), a media-query contradiction with no
reference corpus needed (rec 7), a motion model that scores 0.645 against a 0.953 baseline
(rec 18), a driver viewport exceeding the spoofed screen in 75% of renders, and 185 synthetic
`isTrusted:false` clicks that the product exists to prevent.

clark's own 0.2.0 post-mortem is the transferable lesson and the most useful artifact in the
sweep: **four patches present in 0.1.0 silently never fired at runtime**. A patch can apply,
build, and pass an args-level golden while doing nothing. cuttle's golden has the same shape and
therefore the same blind spot. Recommendation 20 (an adversarial detection lane) is the
structural answer; recommendation 33's self-check is the cheap version.

---

## 6. Proposed and rejected, with reasons

Recorded so nobody re-proposes them. These came out of the theme sweep and were cut by the
adversarial critic or by cuttle's own constraints.

| Proposal | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| Cherry-pick clark's `0047-suppress-cdc-globals` | Makes `Object.defineProperty` a non-native function. A worse tell than what it hides. |
| Raise `PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_TIMEOUT_ACTION` to 30000 as the typing fix | Makes every blocked-page failure take 30s to surface, directly against recs 1 and 4. Fix the cap instead. |
| A parallel CDP-screenshot endpoint in the daemon | playwright-cli's `screenshot` rebuilt one layer down, and structurally cannot see the chrome that motivated the request. |
| Per-eTLD+1 seed derivation (Brave-style farbling) | Protects a real human from cross-site tracking. cuttle's seeds are synthetic identities whose *value* is being identical across sessions so logins persist. Would break persistent profiles outright. |
| Strict per-client tab binding | agent-browser needs it because it multiplexes sessions inside one browser. cuttle multiplexes seeds; the answer to two agents is two seeds. The non-strict half (remember the target, re-select on reconnect) stays defensible. |
| Per-seed Xvnc displays | N framebuffers, N window managers and N encoders to serve a surface that is off by default. |
| An MCP server for cuttle | A second surface to keep in parity, for a tool whose whole model is "attach your own driver". Recs 15 and 22 make cuttle wrappable by whoever wants one. |
| A cuttle-side interstitial auto-dismiss registry | A proxy clicking consent buttons the agent never asked for, at machine timing, on pages the user did not authorize writes to. |

One upstream fact worth recording as strategic rather than technical: playwright-cli's
maintainers **explicitly declined to build cuttle**. PR `#319` proposed Xvfb plus noVNC plus a
container and was rejected point by point - "client is responsible for the sandbox, not the
tool". The wedge is durable.

---

## 7. Blind spots: what this research structurally cannot see

The session audit's own critic produced this, and it is the most important section for anyone
reading the ranking as truth. All nine biases run in the same direction: **toward things agents
tried, and away from things that were never attempted or never observed.**

1. **The human half of the loop is unrecorded.** One X display, shared by every seed, no auth,
   nothing logged. Keystrokes, tab closes, dialog dismissals, a cookie banner cleared by hand -
   none of it appears. The corpus holds only what the human narrated afterwards, and those are the
   angry cases. The larger quiet class is the human silently unblocking something and the agent's
   next command simply working, which every transcript scores as a cuttle success. **Every apparent
   success rate here is inflated by an unknown amount of unrecorded human labour.**

2. **Non-attempts leave no residue.** A broken verb produces an error and a cluster. A *missing*
   verb produces nothing. One upload gap surfaced only because the human happened to ask "wasn't
   there any place in form to submit docs?" - without that question the session reads as clean.
   Missing surface, agent ignorance of existing surface, and "not needed" are indistinguishable in
   a transcript.

3. **Stealth outcomes can never be in a transcript.** No site announces a raised risk score, a
   degraded page, a shadowban, or a challenge queued for three visits from now. The corpus can
   count 185 `isTrusted:false` clicks; it cannot observe one consequence of them. The proof is the
   0.11.3 main-world probe leak: for that release's whole life it emitted observable evaluates on
   every click, and the cost landed in transcripts as "a captcha appeared and the agent fought it
   for ten calls", filed against the site. **Whole categories of cuttle-caused failure are sitting
   in this corpus mislabeled as site behaviour.**

4. **Transcript-mined root causes are agents' beliefs, and about cuttle's internals those beliefs
   are demonstrably wrong.** The background-tab bug wore four disguises for two weeks. Sharpest
   case: `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled` makes `hasFocus()` return true, so the daemon
   actively falsifies the diagnostic the agent reaches for - and the agent's confidently wrong
   conclusion is then mined as evidence.

5. **Abandonment reads as competence.** Three screenshot timeouts and a session stops taking
   screenshots for good. Giving up is one line; what the agent would have done with a working
   primitive is unwritten. Worse, once a workaround is written into a user's own CLAUDE.md, the
   friction is paid on every future run while generating no new evidence forever.

6. **Silent-wrong-answer sessions look identical to correct ones.** 15 sessions used cuttle as an
   authenticated cookie jar and dispatched zero input events. If a session had expired or a lazy
   loader never fired, the output is still shaped correctly. The corpus has exactly one datapoint
   here - a wrong figure the human caught by eye - and that is a datapoint about *that human's
   checking habits*, not about the error rate.

7. **Losses that cross a session boundary have no owner.** The cookie-capture defect was found by
   source review, not by any session failing, because the consequence lands next week in a
   different session where a re-login is narrated as routine.

8. **The multi-client collision base rate is unknowable.** The one caught pair was caught only
   because both narrated confusion in the same minute. The ordinary collision is one weird result,
   retried and attributed to the page.

9. **Felt cost is not call count.** A person watching thirty chips get re-added at 0.5s per click
   on their own live account experiences something the call count does not capture - including the
   moment where the next click might be Delete. Every near-interrupt, and every task the human
   simply did himself, leaves nothing behind.

**Two things excluded from the ranking rather than ranked low**, because the corpus cannot
evidence them at all: (a) any measurement of cuttle's real detection rate - the only honest
instrument is an external bench; (b) any baseline of what a *good* session looks like, because
nobody clustered successes.

---

## 8. Upstream state, per repo

What shipped since our clones went stale, and the one thing that matters most from each.

**`microsoft/playwright-cli` + `playwright-core`** (207 issues; the PR stream is almost all
version rolls, so the signal is in maintainer replies). The engine under cuttle's default driver
already solves the dialog theme - per-tab `ModalState[]`, actions raced against modal-state
events, `defineTabTool` refusing any tool without `clearsModalState`. That reframes cuttle's
dialog work from "build dialog handling" to "cover the window no driver can reach, and route the
agent to the verbs that already exist". Also confirmed: `--timeout-action` defaults to 5000ms
(which is what makes the 80-rune cap a live bug); snapshot size is deliberately *not* being
optimized ("1M models are sufficiently efficient"); and `connectOverCDP` silently launches a
fresh browser when isolation is left on - the single most dangerous failure mode for cuttle's
premise.

**`vercel-labs/agent-browser`** (575 issues, very high traffic through Aug 2026). The most
complete treatment of blocked pages anywhere: `start_dialog_handler`, and a
`dispatch_mouse_or_dialog` that races the input ack against `javascriptDialogOpening` - because a
synchronous dialog blocks the renderer so the ack *cannot* arrive - with `pending_release`
recovery. Their whole 2026 direction is "stop failing silently": persistent session-to-tab
binding with a structured `tab_gone` code, popups registering in the background with a notice,
`background:true` on target creation to stop stealing OS focus. Also an encrypted credential
vault (`auth save --password-stdin`) whose stated purpose is keeping passwords out of the agent's
context window. And the community independently specified cuttle in issue `#120`: run a stealth
browser exposing CDP and attach to it.

**`CloakHQ/CloakBrowser`** (30.3k stars, ~500 issues, maintainer answers nearly every thread with
root-cause detail). Architecturally cuttle's mirror image - stealth in the binary, humanization in
a Python wrapper that *evaporates* over CDP (`#126`), which is third-party validation of cuttle's
core bet. Most valuable single item is `#507` (see rec 2). Also `#428` (iframe actions resolved
against the top frame), `#329` (settle gate not re-scrolling: "a click landed on nothing while
reporting success"), and `#504` (the external-protocol dialog, with three obvious fixes confirmed
*not* to work).

**`CloakHQ/CloakBrowser-Manager`** (52 issues). The closest structural analogue to cuttle's
container half, and its issue list is a field log of the same seams. Best finding is `#8` (see rec
7). PR `#47` fits the window to the framebuffer post-launch; PR `#50` retreats wholesale from a
noVNC-plus-translation-bridge architecture and adds the affordances cuttle lacks - tab manager,
downloads browser, an idle timeout that correctly does *not* count keepalive pings as traffic,
cold-CDP auto-launch. Its clipboard threads (`#2`, `#7`, `#9`) are the trap list for any
secret-paste path.

**`clark-labs-inc/clark-browser`** (cuttle's fork parent). No community at all - 3 off-topic
issues, 0 PRs, Discussions disabled - so releases and CHANGELOG are the feed. Two patches landed
after the `stealth5` tag cuttle pinned to: `0027` (AnalyserNode noise, closing a *named* bypass of
patch `0026` which cuttle does ship) and `0047` (rejected, see section 6). The most transferable
artifact is the 0.2.0 post-mortem described in 5.3.

**`clearcotelabs/clearcote-browser`** (83 stars, 13 issues, every thread high-signal). The most
advanced engine work in the set and the only project that measures its motion model (see rec 18).
Its `141-media-queries.patch` is the fix for rec 7. Its issue threads are a catalogue of
self-contradiction bugs detectable with no reference corpus: canvas noise on a flat fill, a WebGL2
context advertising WebGL1 extensions, srflx candidates with zero host candidates, client hints
disagreeing with JS. Plus the maintainer's stance that suppression is worse than plumbing, and
that an unoptimised build is itself a timing signal - their DOM benchmark ran 11x slow, with
pure-JS loops as the control that proves it.

---

## 8b. Follow-up found after publication: the locale-override collision

Found on 2026-08-18 while stress-testing the build that followed this research. Recorded
here because the cheap half shipped and the real fix did not.

**Symptom.** The daemon logs `injected CDP command failed: ... Another locale override is
already in effect` - 2,872 times in one long-running container.

**Root cause.** `Emulation.setLocaleOverride` is claimed per DevTools *session* but applied
per renderer *process*. `LocaleController` is a per-process singleton and `UpdateLocale` sets
the process-global ICU default; each session gets its own `InspectorEmulationAgent` with its
own claim flag. So the second and later sessions touching the same renderer are always
refused. cuttle mints a fresh session per attached page per connection, and a driver like
playwright-cli runs one process (one connection) per command, which is where the thousands
come from. The daemon's own keep-alive tab is also a claimant.

**Is it harmful?** Mechanically there is a real path: only the session that *succeeded* holds
the claim, and when it disposes Chrome resets ICU to the embedder locale, while the sessions
that were refused never retry. That would leave a live page reporting the seed locale in
`navigator.language` and `en-US` in `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().locale` - the
exact mismatch the pin exists to prevent. **Attempts to reproduce it failed**: across two
experiments, including forcing co-residency with `window.open`, `Intl` stayed on the seed
locale after the claim owner disconnected, with collisions confirmed in the log. So the
mechanism is established from source; the damage is not demonstrated. One seed was observed
with six renderer processes, so co-residency is hard to guarantee and the negative result is
weak evidence, not a refutation.

**Two fixes that look obvious and are not.** Clearing the override before setting it is
impossible: the guard runs before the empty-locale branch, so a fresh session's release call
is refused for the same reason. Deduping by target or skipping an already-pinned page is a
stealth regression by construction, because the claim is bound to the session, and the state
where a pin is skipped is exactly the state where the previous claim was released.

**What shipped:** the failing method is now named in the log, and this one method/message
pairing is demoted rather than warned, with a test pinning that the demotion cannot widen
into a blanket swallow.

**What has not:** the real fix is to set the ICU default in the engine instead of over CDP,
extending `packages/browser/patches/0005-navigator-languages-from-cli.patch` along the lines
of clearcote's `092-language-locale-coherence.patch` (BSD-3, licence-clean), then deleting the
CDP pin entirely. That covers pages nobody ever attaches to, which the CDP pin never could.
It needs a browser rebuild and a release cycle.

**Check this first.** `--lang=<locale>` is already passed to the browser process, and the
container runs headed, not headless. The code comment claiming `--lang` is "inert headless"
may simply not apply here. Measure `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().locale` on a
fresh non-English seed with the CDP pin removed before writing any C++ - if `--lang` already
does the job, the pin is redundant today and the fix is a deletion.

---

## 9. Watch list

Not worth doing now, worth tracking.

**WebDriver BiDi is the largest genuine blind spot.** Nothing in the sweep touched it, and it is
both the standards-track answer to the dialog theme and a **live risk to cuttle's entire
interception model**. BiDi specifies exactly the design all three drivers converged on
independently (`browsingContext.userPromptOpened` plus `handleUserPrompt`), and answers the
question rec 4 works around with a `userPromptHandler` capability set per prompt type at session
creation. It also defines `input.setFiles` (rec 16 without the chooser race) and
`browsingContext.navigationStarted/Committed` (a navigation barrier without sniffing CDP frames).
The operational risk: Playwright ships a BiDi backend (`src/server/bidi/`), and `wsproxy.go`
proxies CDP only. **If any driver ever attaches over BiDi, every interception cuttle has -
context blocking, humanization, focus pins, locale override, proxy-407 auth - silently no-ops,
with no error and no log.** Minimum action now: detect a BiDi handshake at the proxy and refuse it
loudly rather than passing it through unhumanized.

**Navigation and network barriers.** Passively parsing `Page.frameStartedNavigating` and
`Network.requestWillBeSent`/`loadingFinished` from frames already crossing the proxy, to block a
press into a doomed document and expose `{inflight, quietForMs, lastNavigationAt}`. Correctly
diagnosed and real - the settle gate makes the navigation case *worse*, since a doomed page is not
animating so its rect reads stable. Deferred because rec 2 fixes the sharpest instance of the same
root cause for a fraction of the work.

**Prompt injection as a first-class surface.** Rec 1 puts the framing rule in SKILL.md, which is
the cheap 80%. The remainder is a proper channel audit and a delimiting convention - worth having
before a fourth recommendation pipes page-authored text into the instruction channel.

**Captcha and challenge handling as its own capability.** cuttle's stated reason to exist, with no
verb for "are we on a challenge page", no wait-until-cleared, and no escalate-to-human. Rec 12
(per-frame worlds) is the load-bearing prerequisite, since Turnstile clears on a real click inside
a cross-origin iframe that a root-frame-only world cannot probe. A `cuttle handoff [--until-url]`
that prints the viewer URL and blocks on a condition the daemon can already observe would give an
agent at a 2FA wall one obvious thing to do. Keep it strictly print-and-wait: the moment it clicks
anything, cuttle has become the driver.

**Complete the WebGL story, or change the claim.** Patch 0016 spoofs only the two UNMASKED strings
while the entrypoint launches `--use-angle=swiftshader`, so WebGL2's inline `GL_VERSION`, the
numeric `getParameter` vector and `getSupportedExtensions()` all report SwiftShader under a
discrete-GPU claim. clearcote argues this is unfixable in principle - you cannot make one GPU emit
another GPU's exact pixels in software. At minimum, soften `SKILL.md:464-468`, which currently
tells agents the GPU is coherent when only the *strings* are. Related and worth documenting rather
than discovering: a shared software rasterizer means every seed on a host shares one canvas hash
and is cross-linkable.

**Sources worth a second pass before the next sweep.** patchright and rebrowser-patches (the
implemented answer to the `Runtime.enable` leak, plus the documented list of what it breaks -
`exposeFunction`, `page.on('console')` - which is what a naive port of clearcote's blunt patch
would regress); browser-use's main repo (its watchdog architecture is the shape three themes
independently reinvent, and its WebVoyager/Mind2Web harness is the only thing in the corpus that
would answer "does any of this raise task success rate"); camoufox (a second humanized-cursor model
to score against clearcote's); Steel and Browserbase (Steel is structurally closest to cuttle's
container half; Browserbase's live-view takeover toggle is the shipped version of what several gaps
propose); Playwright's tracing subsystem (`trace.zip` is the mature answer to run observability that
several themes reinvent in slices); and the CDP spec itself - `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` carries
`pointerType`, `force`, `tiltX/Y` and `twist`, **none of which cuttle sets**, so every synthesized
event has a default pressure profile no real pointer produces.

**Cost and density were never measured, by anyone.** Every recommendation here carries an effort
label and no memory or CPU number. Nobody swept how many seeds fit a node, what `/dev/shm` sizing
does, or what SwiftShader costs per tab. The one datapoint in the entire corpus is a playwright-cli
maintainer's "still within the rounding error of your LLM completion", used rhetorically. Recs 4 and
18 both add permanent per-seed work - measure before landing both.

---

## Appendix A - The 47 ranked issues, with evidence

The ranked list from section 3, with the evidence that produced each rank. Session labels
are `S01`..`S46`; the mapping to real identifiers is deliberately not in this repo.

### A1. No write in the stack returns a post-condition: clicks, fills and saves report success while changing nothing, so every session hand-builds a bespoke verification loop after every mutation

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 16 sessions; the single largest call-cost class in the corpus - S01 spent ~400 of 892 calls (46%) answering 'what is actually on the page'

**Evidence.** S01 built an out-of-band protocol - Gravar -> `cuttle downloads` -> grep the form XML - after every single field, because 'Q6 is confirmed still empty in the XML (the click didn't take - custom widget)', and downloaded the same file nine times tracking Chrome's `(3).xml`..`(9).xml` dedup counter by hand. S05: `el.click()` on Google's account chooser returned 'clicked account' with the URL unmoved; a bash loop over freshly-snapshotted refs printed three empty arrows and added zero pages while looking like it ran. S09: a hosting provider Submit no-op'd through three clicks and two full form refills. Corpus-wide 592 snapshot calls against 2749 driver calls - roughly one page re-read per four actions, almost all of it read-back.

**Root cause.** cuttle already computes a settle gate and an aria-toggle post-click verify internally for humanized clicks and discards the verdict; fill/type have no equivalent at all; drivers exit 0 on no-ops. Note this issue covers only the two mechanical cases the daemon can see - input never dispatched, and input dispatched but ignored by the page. The third case the upstream cluster merged in (an HTTP 200 that discarded the write) is mechanically unrelated and is ranked separately at #14.

**Missing capability.** Every cuttle-owned write returns the post-condition instead of a checkmark: 'click landed at (x,y) on <el>, DOM subtree changed / unchanged, active element after = <sel>, field value before -> after, N requests failed'. Extend the existing humanized-click verify to fill/type and surface the verdict the daemon already computes.

### A2. Input dispatched to a non-foreground tab is silently swallowed and background tabs get near-zero rAF, so actionability never settles and lazy loaders never fire - one root cause that wore four different disguises for two weeks

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 7 sessions; 139 timeout-signature results = 4.9% of 2827 browser calls in S16's measured window, and 0 of 8 identical retries ever worked

**Evidence.** S16 measured it on live containers: 'noDefaults:true G/background -> fps 0, click FAIL 15002ms (waiting for element to be visible, enabled and stable)' vs 'noDefaults:false G/background -> fps 61, click OK 851ms'. S05 rediscovered 'The tab is backgrounded (visibilityState: hidden), so Google's UI won't respond' at least four separate times. S02 spent 58 calls concluding 'cuttle's Chromium never fires the lazy RSC requests', told the user 'I have to stop and hand this back to you', then twenty minutes later: 'Found it - document.hidden === true... That was the whole bug - a background tab, not a cuttle limitation.'

**Root cause.** cuttle's base Chrome args omit Playwright's four backgrounding switches, and the connectOverCDP/noDefaults:true attach path cuttle documents skips Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled. The per-page pin shipped in 0.11.4 restores compositing but does not make document.visibilityState 'visible', so IntersectionObserver lazy loaders still never fire and long-hidden tabs still degrade to ~1fps.

**Missing capability.** Extend the pin to page-visibility, pass the renderer anti-backgrounding launch flags, expose per-target foreground/visibilityState in `cuttle status`, and error when Input is dispatched at a target that cannot receive it instead of dropping it silently.

### A3. When the driver's click fails there is no trusted-click escape hatch, so agents escalate to force-click, DOM dispatchEvent and hand-computed coordinates - the exact move that emits isTrusted:false and throws away the product's only reason to exist

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 185 synthetic-click calls across 21 sessions in the 14-day window (verified by SQL), against 968 eval/run-code calls total

**Evidence.** S07 caught itself: 'synthetic events are isTrusted: false, while cuttle's humanized input layer sends real CDP input events... Making dispatchEvent my reflex quietly throws away the stealth property on exactly the anti-bot sites where we need it.' S05 rebuilt clicking from scratch - 27 evals whose only job was getBoundingClientRect, each followed by `mousemove X Y; mousedown; mouseup`, coordinates stale on any reflow - and then proved the cost: `el.click()` on Google's account chooser reported success with the URL unmoved, and only a real mouse press advanced it. S09: 'roughly 30 of the 64 driver calls are --raw eval DOM surgery, including every Submit click'. S13 pressed a Save button with an injected `b.click()` because no ref existed.

**Root cause.** cuttle's humanized input is reachable only through a driver verb that frequently cannot resolve or act on the element (see #2, #4, #22), and cuttle offers no native alternative - so the only available escape hatch is the one that defeats humanization. The daemon cannot even see it happen: the humanizer sits on Input.dispatchMouseEvent / dispatchKeyEvent / insertText (humanize.go:136-138) and anything through Runtime.evaluate is invisible to it.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle click <selector\|x,y>` that resolves, scrolls into view, foregrounds the target and dispatches humanized real Input in one call; plus a hard SKILL.md rule that dispatchEvent/force-click through cuttle is forbidden, paired with that alternative so the rule is followable.

### A4. The accessibility snapshot omits or mislabels exactly the controls real authenticated apps use (Save buttons, OAuth links, expanders, Angular, LWC, legacy WebForms and shadow-DOM widgets), so raw-JS DOM extraction became the universal escape hatch

`critical` | layer: `mixed` | 10 sessions; the most-repeated workaround in the corpus and the direct upstream cause of #3

**Evidence.** S13: `snapshot \| rg -i 'button.*save\|publish\|Save'` returned only 'Published on June 30, 2022'; a raw eval over document.querySelectorAll('button') found ' Save'. S14: ~20 of ~35 a consumer banking web app calls were raw evals because the a11y layer was useless on legacy WebForms. S07: 'The form lives in a CRM platform LWC shadow DOM - document.querySelector can't see it (Playwright locators can)' - the agent hand-wrote a shadowRoot walker. S15: `find 'Continue with GitHub' \| rg 'ref='` returned four nested generics and no link ref.

**Root cause.** The a11y tree is the only structured page view cuttle's documented drivers offer and it is lossy on the widget families that matter. There is no blessed structured-extraction path, so agents fall to page-context JS - which is blind to shadow DOM and bypasses humanization.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle extract --selector ... --format json` returning form values keyed by nearest stable id/name/label, piercing shadow DOM and spanning frames; plus a documented rule that eval is for reads only and that locators pierce shadow DOM where document.querySelector does not.

### A5. Multiple clients share one browser with no visibility, ownership or lease - a human or a second agent silently clobbers the agent's work, and the surfaced error always blames the selector

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 7 sessions observed; true base rate unknowable (see blind spot 8) - the one caught pair was caught only because both narrated confusion in the same minute

**Evidence.** S05 and S06 ran against the SAME container simultaneously on 2026-08-07 and were each other's invisible saboteur. S05: 'Stopping here - we're both driving the same browser and it's clobbering the work... That also explains the three Create connection clicks that did nothing' - task handed back to the human, the corpus's only user interrupt. S06: 'another session has opened tabs in this browser and stolen document focus, which makes execCommand a no-op' - after it had already corrupted a live build config into `[v0.14.6 array][v0.14.5 array]`. S12 found the deployment-scale version: local dev and prod compute the same deterministic seed and land on the same single-replica Chrome, 'either side's browser.close() can kill the other's page'.

**Root cause.** cuttle multiplexes CDP but exposes no notion of who else is attached or which targets are claimed; seeds are a small deterministic pool with no per-client namespacing; `cuttle status` says nothing about attached clients. cuttle's own `--context` feature is the fix and was used once corpus-wide.

**Missing capability.** Per-attach target ownership or an advisory lease, attached-client count and foreground owner in `cuttle status`, per-client seed namespacing, and a briefing line telling an agent to claim a context when another session may be driving.

### A6. Discoverability, not capability, is the binding constraint: shipped affordances that exactly solve top-ranked problems went essentially unused, so the corpus's apparent demand for ~25 new verbs is partly a demand for a findable briefing

`critical` | layer: `skill-docs` | Corpus-wide. Verified usage in the window: `cuttle up` 37 calls but `cuttle open` 2, `cuttle skill` 5, `--context` 13 (concentrated in dev/audit sessions), `cuttle context` 1. run-code was used 201 times but in only 19 of 72 page-driving sessions

**Evidence.** `--context` / `context ls\|add` is the exact fix for the #5 collision incident, is never mentioned in the `cuttle up` briefing, and was reached for once in 14 days. S05 read the driver's own SKILL.md documenting run-code and made zero run-code calls in 183 browser calls; its own audit put ~65 of those as collapsible to ~5. S07 on agent-browser batch: 'That's the thing I claimed was structural.' S28 truncated the `cuttle up` briefing to `tail -2` to save context, discarding the routing rules entirely. S01 opened a non-cuttle browser for a second identity and the user had to ask 'are you using cuttle? or some magic stuff yourslef?' before it learned `cuttle up --name` exists.

**Root cause.** The briefing teaches attachment and names drivers; it does not name the features that resolve the failures agents actually hit, and it is long enough that agents truncate it. Any remedy list read off this corpus will over-weight new verbs because a missing verb and an unfindable verb are indistinguishable in a transcript.

**Missing capability.** A short, stable `cuttle up` briefing (short enough that nobody truncates it) that names the existing escape hatches against the symptoms that trigger them - second identity -> `cuttle up --name`, another driver present -> `--context`, slow discrete loop -> run-code/batch, bulk entry on your own account -> `--humanize=false` - with the full guide behind `cuttle skill`.

### A7. The operational facts that decide success live nowhere durable, so every session re-derives the same mechanics and then writes a private cuttle playbook into the user's own repo - and cuttle's embedded SKILL.md carries claims that are factually false about the daemon

`critical` | layer: `skill-docs` | 11 sessions re-deriving; 3 sessions acting on false SKILL.md claims; three different user repos now carry hand-rolled cuttle documentation

**Evidence.** The same four driver mechanics were independently re-derived in at least five sessions: run-code is Node not the page, run-code needs an `async page => {}` wrapper, long text needs insertText, click sometimes needs raw coordinates. S10 wrote them into `pjs/career/docs/SCAM_REPORTING.md` under 'Driving these forms with cuttle'; S01 wrote a 34-line block into `pjs/finances/CLAUDE.md`; S02 rewrote `tools/a professional social network-access/CLAUDE.md`. The humans said it directly: 'have you checked full skills/docs of both tools? could it be that we are just not using them in the most efficient way?' (S07 - the agent's own answer: 'Roughly 12-15 of my ~30 browser calls were avoidable'). On the false-claims half, S16: 'internal/cli/SKILL.md:200 claims off-centre clicks (the code removed that at humanize.go:398-403), and it says typing is rewritten into human-paced motion when fill on both drivers goes through Input.insertText, which the humanizer never sees.'

**Root cause.** SKILL.md is //go:embed'ed and is therefore shipped behaviour, but nothing keeps its behavioural claims under test against the daemon's code paths, and the load-bearing operational rules are carried by neither the briefing nor SKILL.md.

**Missing capability.** Move the handful of load-bearing rules into the embedded SKILL.md, and add a test asserting SKILL.md's behavioural claims against the daemon (keystroke counts on fill, click offsets, flag pass-through) so docs drift cannot land silently.

### A8. There is no wait-for-condition primitive, so agents pad every action with blind sleeps and hand-roll polling loops, and 'is it stuck or just slow' is never answerable

`high` | layer: `mixed` | 661 browser calls in the window carry a literal sleep (verified); 14 sessions; >1 hour of pure wall clock, one session at 58% of calls

**Evidence.** S06: 32 sleeps totalling 894s plus 7 hand-rolled polling loops (~40 min), including `for i in 1..8; do sleep 40; playwright-cli goto ".../tests/<id>?p=$i"`, and one attempt rejected by the harness ('Blocked: sleep 150 followed by: curl'). S05: 85 of 183 browser calls contain a sleep, 354s total. S04: 76 sleeps (~264s) despite SKILL.md's own rule 'Never sleep; wait for a condition' - because it had navigated inside eval, leaving no driver-side navigation to wait on.

**Root cause.** No wait primitive at the cuttle layer and no confidence in the drivers'; combined with humanized latency and cold starts, agents cannot distinguish designed slowness from a hang, so they guess a duration on every interaction. Several sessions also appended cache-busting query params because there is no hard-reload verb.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle wait --tab N --until '<js predicate>' \| --url-matches \| --settled --timeout`, a documented hard reload, and a stated per-action latency budget so agents stop padding a humanized click with a 9-second sleep.

### A9. The dominant real use of cuttle is authenticated data extraction through a tab that carries the user's cookies, and it has no verb - so every session reverse-engineers the site's private API and re-derives its auth flow from scratch

`high` | layer: `cli` | 15 sessions; in three of them (S36, S37, S40) not one CDP input event was ever sent - zero clicks, zero typing

**Evidence.** The identical incantation appears in S23, S37, S29, S33, S27 and S34: `--raw eval "async () => { const t=(await (await fetch('https://api.a freelance marketplace/auth/refresh-token',{method:'POST',credentials:'include'})).json()).token; ... }" \| jq`. S17 spent 24 of 29 calls on in-page fetch of a link-aggregator site .json because 'plain curl is bot-blocked', carrying a 400-character shell-quoted blob as session state across three compactions. S02's user pushed back directly: 'every time i ask you to check a professional social network, do you need to run cuttle for that? could you store the token in here somehow?'

**Root cause.** cuttle's value today is 'a logged-in cookie jar reachable over CDP', and the briefing names drivers and attach commands but never names this use case, so everything above that layer is the agent's own JS re-derived per session and per site.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle fetch <url> [--json] [--out file]` and `cuttle eval --file script.js` executing in the seed's authenticated page context, returning un-escaped output and streaming large results to a file. The most-used capability in the batch and the only one with no verb.

### A10. Agents have no cheap visual ground truth and no rule for when to look, and the one capture path they do have is unreliable - so page-state questions get answered from DOM reads that lie, while the sessions that do screenshot mostly waste them

`critical` | layer: `mixed` | 55 of 72 page-driving sessions (76%) took zero driver screenshots; 113 total in the window, 22 of them in a single session that its own audit calls pure waste; 6 sessions hit hard capture failures

**Evidence.** S01: 628 driver calls broke down as snapshot 245, eval 208, click 69, screenshot 6, and the user finally asked 'are you able to screenshot the whole page so that you won't be guessing so much missing stuff from what is actually on screen?' then caught a wrong tax figure himself ('and check the screenshot, you did some shit' - campo 61 read 9.819,81). S05: zero screenshots in 183 calls, 27 evals whose only job was getBoundingClientRect. S17: zero screenshots in 29 calls while the human pasted [Image #1]-[Image #12]. On the reliability half, S04 hit three identical 'TimeoutError: Timeout 5000ms exceeded... taking page screenshot - waiting for fonts to load' on three unrelated sites and then abandoned visual verification for the session; S22's capture printed 'ok' and wrote no file; S09 used `--path=` (the flag is `--filename`) and got three byte-identical 251.9K PNGs of a virtualized list; S08 needed `--hires` plus a hand-written PIL 5x upscale to read a 140x60 captcha.

**Root cause.** Two coupled defects. cuttle has no capture of its own, so visual truth is inherited driver behaviour whose page-screenshot path waits on document.fonts.ready - which does not settle in cuttle's container, almost certainly because of the metric-renamed win/mac font packs the image installs. And nothing anywhere states when an image is the cheapest answer, so the distribution is bimodal: most sessions never look, one session looks reflexively and learns nothing.

**Missing capability.** A daemon-side capture (CDP Page.captureScreenshot with clip + deviceScaleFactor, no font gate) echoing an absolute path and a content hash, exposed as `cuttle shot [--tab N] [--full] [--element sel]`; plus a SKILL.md decision rule naming the four situations where an image is the cheapest answer (two consecutive no-op actions, an icon-only control, a control that reports enabled but does nothing, any visual-judgment request) and the one where it is not (routine state read - use a scoped text extract).

### A11. Tabs are addressed by positional integer in a browser other actors freely mutate, with no agent-owned tab, no stable handle and no cuttle tab verb at all

`high` | layer: `cli` | 16 sessions; one audit counted ~115 calls corpus-wide, and one session spent 33 calls (18%) purely on tab orientation

**Evidence.** S17: an eval landed on the human's own tab and returned `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<', "<!doctype "... is not valid JSON` - twice - and the compaction summary had to carry 'my tab is tab 1, tab 0 is HIS' as standing state, which inverted when the human closed a tab. S15: `tab-close 3; tab-close 1; tab-close 0` in one command left four tabs with reshuffled indices. S05: an eval intended for a Notion dialog executed against a a SaaS console page and returned a React stack trace the agent did not recognise as wrong-page output. Aggravated by attach hygiene: S27 attached onto `0: (current) [](https://a media-index site/signup.php)` and auto-snapshotted it; S15 attached into a browser holding `0: [Logout - a consumer banking web app](.../a session-timeout page)` and `3: [a consumer banking app](https://a consumer banking web app/logged-out)`.

**Root cause.** cuttle exposes no tab/target concept - enumeration arrives only as a driver side effect, indices are positional, cuttle's own immortal keep-alive about:blank occupies an ordinal that shifts everything after it, and attach binds to whatever page the last human or agent left focused.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle open --name <label>` returning a stable target id, `cuttle tabs` listing id/url/title/foreground/owner, driver commands pinnable to an id (or an `--expect-url` guard that fails rather than executing against whatever is at that ordinal now), and a dedicated working tab handed to each attaching client.

### A12. Native JS dialogs are invisible to the agent and unowned by cuttle: commands stall silently behind a pending dialog, accepting one destroys unsaved client-side state with no warning, and a dialog held by one driver wedges the tab for every other client

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions, 6 episodes - but each episode is catastrophic rather than costly, and dialogs are per-driver-session so cross-client wedges cannot be counted

**Evidence.** S01: four separate 'Leave site?' episodes on a completed tax declaration; 'The draft is still alive - the navigation is stuck on the pending dialog' followed later by 'The navigation had already fired, so the in-browser draft is gone'; the user: 'can you do those changes without reuploading the file now? i'm tired of you getting stuck on Leave site? screens'. Only 4 dialog-accept and 1 dialog-dismiss calls exist in the whole 892-call session - every other dialog was discovered by inference after commands stopped working. S02, mid-task driver switch: 't2 is stuck - likely a pending dialog held by the playwright session', post-mortem 'beforeunload dialog stuck tab t2 (agent-browser "tab is not responding"): cleared via playwright-cli dialog-dismiss + session close'.

**Root cause.** cuttle never intercepts Page.javascriptDialogOpening. Dialog state lives in whichever CDP client opened it, so one driver can orphan a dialog on a browser other clients depend on, and the victim gets a generic 'tab is not responding'. Nothing warns that accepting a beforeunload discards unsaved page state.

**Missing capability.** Daemon-owned dialog interception: pending dialogs (type + message) in `cuttle status` and prefixed onto any command that would otherwise hang, `cuttle dialog list\|accept\|dismiss` independent of the driver session that triggered it, and an explicit warning when the pending dialog is beforeunload.

### A13. cuttle owns no secret-injection path, so the driver's attach-time substitution types unknown names literally into live fields, cannot reach run-code at all, and three sessions independently reinvented the same native-value-setter DOM hack

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 8 sessions across the two failure shapes; two human credential takeovers, one wrongly-aborted task, two burned TOTP attempts

**Evidence.** S08: `fill f7e182 HC_PASS` returned 'len=7' with no warning and a 24-char control secret landed as len=11; the agent concluded 'The secret-substitution path drops characters - a driver bug' and aborted the whole task to a human handoff before finding the truth: 'HC_PASS is 7 characters, TEST_SECRET is 11 - exactly the lengths that landed. Nothing was truncated; it typed the literal variable names.' S01 hit the same on a government portal ('Campo demasiado pequeno') and the user entered the credentials themselves, with visible frustration at having to re-explain the flow. S16 established the complementary hole: 'playwright-cli's secrets substitution doesn't work inside run-code at all (no process.env in the sandbox)', so S11 inlined a live OTP into argv: `run-code "async page => { await page.keyboard.type('119389', { delay: 150 }); }"`. The reinvented hack appears verbatim in S23, S26, S27 and S33.

**Root cause.** Secret substitution is a driver env-var feature evaluated once at session create, applying only to that driver's discrete fill/type verbs. Nothing errors on an unknown name, nothing verifies redaction is armed on an already-attached session, and a time-bounded secret cannot be refreshed without a new attach (S04 accumulated sessions bt, bt2, bt3 against one browser in 90 seconds).

**Missing capability.** Daemon-owned secret injection at the CDP Input layer, resolved at use time - a named secret any driver can reference in any call shape, delivered as real keystrokes, never crossing argv or the transcript - with a hard error on an unknown name and `cuttle secret set NAME --from-command 'op item get ... --otp'` for time-bounded values.

### A14. Nothing observes the network or console, so agents monkey-patch window.fetch from inside the page, grep console log files by mtime, and download the site's own JS bundles to find out why an action failed - including the whole class where an HTTP 200 silently discarded the write

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 7 sessions; the 200-that-did-nothing case is the third mechanism the #1 cluster wrongly absorbed and no daemon-side settle verdict can ever catch it

**Evidence.** S04 rewrote the page's networking stack to spy on itself - `window.fetch=async (...a)=>{...window.__net.push(o.method+' '+u+' :: '+String(o.body))...}` plus XMLHttpRequest.open/send - then grepped the app's bundles from inside the page to recover an enum (`five_eight_years` -> `eight_or_more_years`) after three guessed values 400'd; separately its skills PATCH 'was accepted but ignored' - HTTP 200, zero change, caught only by habit. S13 had to confirm `POST /user/availability => [200]` from a request log. S09's a hosting provider Submit failure (a 403 on /api/jwt) was found only by `ls -t .playwright-cli/console-*.log \| head -1 \| xargs grep -iE '403'`, and every result reported '183 console errors' on a professional social network so the counter was useless as a signal. S06 could not inspect a redirect chain, substituted an in-page fetch with different cookie semantics, and committed a wrong 'stale cache' conclusion into a project journal.

**Root cause.** cuttle proxies the whole CDP Network and Runtime domains and retains none of it; drivers expose an unfiltered request log and a raw console-error count that large SPAs baseline in the hundreds.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle net --tail` / `cuttle console --since-last-action` per seed: failed requests with status, redirect chains with final URL, request/response bodies for the action just performed, and only console entries new since the previous action, classified rather than counted.

### A15. Session state is lost silently by four independent routes that all return err == nil, and the standard driver call (browser.newContext) puts pages exactly where capture and restore cannot see them

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions, all found by source review or infra work rather than by a session failing - the consequence lands in a later session as a routine re-login (blind spot 7)

**Evidence.** S03: 'storage.GetCookies() is called with no BrowserContextID, so it resolves to the default context only... a capture that returns err == nil (so the if !ok bail never fires), overwrites the prior good snapshot last-writer-wins, and then pool.go:628 deletes the profile dir. Cookies for that context are lost on every capture; its localStorage *is* captured, so the snapshot is left internally inconsistent.' S12: 'browser.newContext() creates an incognito context... Cuttle's own code says its restore path cannot reach it', plus '/data on no volume in either compose or helm' - the advertised persistent-logins feature quietly inert in production. Fourth route: idle reap discards the profile because --keep-profile defaults false.

**Root cause.** Every loss path is invisible at the moment it happens, and cuttle has no way to tell a client that the context it just created is outside the state boundary.

**Missing capability.** Union cookies across all contexts, refuse to overwrite a good snapshot with an empty one, and warn at the point of loss - 'your login will not survive because X' - including when a client creates a context the restore path cannot reach and when a reap is about to discard a profile.

### A16. Daemon and Chrome death is invisible to attached clients: a panic surfaces as a bare ECONNREFUSED or a 502 whose body parses as success, a vanished page carries no attribution, and `cuttle status`'s log tail is 20 lines of dbus noise

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 5 sessions; two releases shipped carrying a live crash because the harness was green while the daemon it drove had died

**Evidence.** S03: 'My earlier confirmed fixed was wrong: I checked at 23:49, and the daemon panicked at 23:51:20, after the harness finished' - the panic (`chromedp.NewContext.func1() chromedp.go:199`) found only by reading pod exit state. S07 lost a filled wizard step to 'Daemon process exited with code 1 [PlaywrightError: connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:9222]' while `cuttle status`'s entire 20-line log window was repeats of '[ERROR:dbus/bus.cc:405] Failed to connect to the bus'. S12 spent hours and three subagents on 'Target page, context or browser has been closed' while 'Cuttle's own logs show the a link-aggregator site Chromes are not crashing - clean disconnected + state captured ~2s after connect', and separately: 'cuttle answers a dead Chrome with a 502 whose body is valid JSON, so it degrades to chrome ?, unknown persona instead of naming the failure.'

**Root cause.** No health or liveness surface a driving agent can poll, no per-teardown attribution (client closeTarget vs transport drop vs idle reap vs crash), a status log tail biased toward benign Chromium noise, and error responses shaped identically to success.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle status` reporting daemon uptime, restart/panic count with stack, and last-capture outcomes; per-page teardown attribution surfaced in the CDP error path; error bodies that cannot be parsed as the success shape; and a log tail biased toward panic/fatal/exit lines.

### A17. Redaction is driver-side, all-or-nothing and destructive: it shreds unrelated output when secrets are short, blocks reading legitimate page data, and secrets still leak through snapshots, URLs in retry logs and the transcript
