`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 5 sessions; one real credential rotation, one exposed live session key, two OTPs in plaintext

**Evidence.** S26: `snapshot \| grep -iE 'error\|textbox'` returned `- textbox "Password..." [ref=e28]: t2J6bXyCQumuF!cukv` - the standard way to diagnose a failed login is a credential exfiltration path. S14 produced a rotation-requiring incident: with per-digit secrets loaded, output came back as `Console: <secret>MBCP_MC_<secret>MBCP_MC_5</secret></secret> errors` and even refs were redacted (`textbox "5ª" [ref=e<secret>MBCP_P5</secret>]`), so the agent detached to read the page - and that snapshot printed three of the user's seven multicanal digits in plaintext; in the same session the human's SMS OTP entered the transcript because there is no hand-in path. S08 leaked a live session key through a routine retry log: '- waiting for "https://z-library.sk/?remix_userid=...&remix_userkey=25039df9..." navigation to finish'. The inverse direction is also unowned: S05 hand-built `getsec.js` then a `createTreeWalker` variant with a `/^(secret_\|ntn_)/` match piped through jq into a `chmod 600` file to read a secret out of a page, and S18 scraped a PAT into a shell variable piped to `gh secret set` with correctness resting entirely on remembering not to echo it.

**Root cause.** Redaction is a naive global substring replace over all driver output, armed once at attach - simultaneously too broad (destroys refs, timestamps, counts) and too narrow (misses snapshot input values and credential-shaped URL params).

**Missing capability.** Daemon-side, value-scoped, always-on masking of exactly the values cuttle injected; snapshot-side redaction of password/one-time-code inputs and credential-shaped URL params; `cuttle secret prompt` so a human can hand over an OTP without it entering the transcript; and a sanctioned read-out path that writes a scraped credential to a file instead of through the model's context.

### A18. There is no upload path into the containerized browser: the driver's upload verb is gated on an already-armed file chooser, fails silently when it is not, and nobody documents whether a path resolves on the host or in the container

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 5 sessions; one permanently incomplete legal filing and one wrong-widget upload on a live account. Under-counted by construction - S07 simply never tried, and that only surfaced because the human asked

**Evidence.** S07: 'On the documents: yes, there was an upload field, and I missed it' - by the time the human asked ('and wasn't there any place in form to submit docs?') the wizard had locked the a national tax id with `attachmentsJSON: ""`, forcing the document out of band by email. S02 ran `docker cp /tmp/kc-logo.png cuttle:/tmp/...`, then `agent-browser upload` reported success while a hand-written probe returned `{files:0}`; only `page.setInputFiles` with a Mac path worked, making the docker cp dead work. S04 got `Error: The tool "browser_file_upload" can only be used when there is related modal state present`, then guessed a control and fired a PDF at the cover-image uploader on the user's live account. S01 hit the mirror error: 'Tool browser_snapshot does not handle the modal state.'

**Root cause.** cuttle owns the downloads direction and not its mirror. Upload is per-driver behaviour keyed to modal state, with no way to name the target input and no documented path-resolution rule for a browser that runs in a container.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle upload <selector-or-index> <local-file>` that stages the file and sets it via CDP DOM.setFileInputFiles regardless of modal state, returning the file name and byte count as the page sees it; plus one SKILL.md line stating which side paths resolve on.

### A19. In-page overlays, modals, toasts and validation messages are not perceivable - there is no 'what is on screen / what is blocking this click' read, so agents dump DOM text and guess

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 9 sessions; worst single episode 20 tool calls on one silently-refusing button

**Evidence.** S07 burned 20 calls on a Continue button that failed silently - 'No request fires and no console error, so client-side validation is silently refusing' - and eventually fetched the site's own LWC bundle and grepped it to find 'Address * - Minimum 3 characters and maximum 35'. S21's first click died on a backdrop visible only in the timeout body: '<div class="fixed inset-0 z-50 ... backdrop-blur-sm"> intercepts pointer events'. S10 queried [role=alert] for a validation error and got the cookie-consent banner. S22's submit confirmation was a self-closing alertdialog the agent caught by luck, then had to prove the submission via a a webmail client receipt.

**Root cause.** No cuttle-level page-state primitive. The a11y snapshot lists overlay content without indicating it covers the page, and transient UI is gone by the time a snapshot polls for it.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle look` / `cuttle overlays` reporting the top layer, the element actually under the intended click point, rendered validation text with shadow DOM flattened, plus a short rolling per-tab log of dialog/toast/navigation events so 'what appeared in the last 10s' is answerable after the fact.

### A20. Humanized typing is slow, capped and silently bypassed above the cap, and it outruns the driver's fixed 5s action timeout - producing hard TimeoutErrors on writes that actually succeeded, on live accounts

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 7 sessions on the latency/cap half, 2 on the false-timeout half; several minutes of blocking wall clock per long field

**Evidence.** S16 measured the cliff exactly: 'short (16) -> 2537ms, 159ms/char; at cap (80) -> 11996ms, 150ms/char; over cap (140) -> 8ms, 0ms/char' - confirmed in source at humanize.go:156 (`insertTextMaxRunes = 80`) and humanize.go:592, where anything longer commits through insertText unhumanized with no signal. S01's 33-row IBAN loop ran 16:19:54 -> 16:24:26 for ~950 characters and the BIC column could not be filled at all, so the agent edited the XML offline and re-uploaded - which triggered the Leave-site dialog that wiped the live draft. S10 routed a 3386-char body through `page.keyboard.insertText`, i.e. 'the fields that most need to look human were filled by the least human path'. S11 hit the false failure twice on the user's live profile: 'TimeoutError: Timeout 5000ms exceeded ... fill("Co-Founder, Baton...")' for 56 characters, where 'The fills actually landed (humanized typing just outran the CLI's 5s report window)' - a naive retry would have doubled the text.

**Root cause.** ~130-160ms/char with an 80-rune cap and a silent fallback to one unhumanized edit, with nothing negotiating the driver's default action timeout against that pacing and nothing documenting the arithmetic.

**Missing capability.** A blessed bulk-text path with a stated stealth tradeoff (chunked humanized runs, or an explicit per-command opt-out), a daemon warning when a fill falls back to insertText, and cuttle extending or negotiating the attached driver's action timeout when humanization is on - failing which SKILL.md must state chars x ~130ms and the rule 'on a fill timeout, read back before retrying, never re-fill'.

### A21. Humanization coverage is narrow and invisible, and the humanize decision is baked at `cuttle up` so it cannot be revised once the agent learns what kind of work the task is

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 6 sessions on the coverage half, 3 on the toggle half; `--humanize` appears in 176 commands but no session ever changed it mid-task

**Evidence.** Confirmed in source: the humanizer intercepts exactly Input.dispatchMouseEvent, Input.dispatchKeyEvent and Input.insertText (humanize.go:136-138); everything through Runtime.evaluate is invisible to it and produces isTrusted=false, while SKILL.md:199-206 claimed typing was 'rewritten into human-paced motion'. S16 measured the prefilter bug across releases: 0.11.3 `fill()` took 15-19ms with 0 keydowns, 0.11.4 took 1.1s with 10 keydowns for the identical value. S01 shows the scale of the invisible bypass: 65 of its eval calls performed the actual clicks and typing via .click()/dispatchEvent/nativeInputValueSetter, none of which the daemon ever saw. On the toggle half, S04 paid ~0.5s/click for ~50 interactions on the user's own a freelance marketplace profile - five minutes to re-add ~30 skill chips with no adversary present - because switching humanize off means --recreate, discarding a session that cost a password, two failed TOTP attempts and four attach cycles.

**Root cause.** The humanizer sits on three CDP methods with no signal to the caller when an action routes around it, and the humanize flag is a launch-time property of the container rather than of the connection.

**Missing capability.** Report humanization coverage per action (or warn when a page mutates without dispatched Input); per-connection or per-command humanize control (`?humanize=0` on the CDP attach URL alongside the existing `?fingerprint=`); and humanize state reported in `cuttle status` and the attach briefing.

### A22. The driver's actionability gate never settles on animated SPA and consent UIs, and a11y refs are single-use - together these are why two sessions abandoned high-level verbs permanently and fell to coordinates

`high` | layer: `mixed` | 6 sessions (actionability) + 7 sessions (stale refs); S01 ran snapshot:click at exactly 1:1 (261:261) because every targeted action needed a fresh snapshot first

**Evidence.** S06 told the user proactively 'a SaaS console buttons never pass Playwright's stability check (some persistent animation), so I'm clicking via DOM dispatch'. S07 on a OneTrust banner: 'waiting for element to be visible, enabled and stable' x4. S05's Google consent flow: two ref/role clicks timed out and the entire remainder of the OAuth flow ran on raw coordinates. On refs: S04 got `Error: Ref f6e811 not found in the current page snapshot` mid-form-fill leaving `year_from=""`; S05 saw `click f12e539` -> 'element was detached from the DOM, retrying' -> 'Ref f12e493 not found', with prefixes drifting e... / f12e... / f2e... between frames; S27 found the same login form's refs were e35 in one session and f7e35 in another, so no known flow can be cached.

**Root cause.** A stability heuristic designed for static pages applied to perpetually-animating UI, plus a per-invocation process model that re-mints refs on every snapshot, with errors that name the element rather than the reason and no distinction between 'ref stale' and 'element absent'.

**Missing capability.** A cuttle-side click that keeps trusted input while skipping the driver's stability gate (see #3); auto-resnapshot on a stale ref or stable handles that survive a re-render; and a SKILL.md rule that refs are valid only within one chained command, with a symptom-to-driver routing hint ('two consecutive actionability timeouts on an animated SPA -> switch driver, same browser, no state lost').

### A23. Nothing reports which element has focus or what a field's value became, so widget-specific semantics fail invisibly and produce syntactically plausible wrong values on live systems

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 6 sessions; two near-miss writes of wrong data to live systems, one of them a legally-binding tax form

**Evidence.** S01: campo 61 was AT-prefilled with 9,81; the agent typed 9,81 and the field became 9.819,81 - caught only by the human's eye. Same session: a fill 'landed in the portal's global search box' and cost 52 calls. S14: `click e89` 'actually focused SMS box 5', putting the first three OTP digits in boxes 5-7 (`"<redacted-id>"`), and `fill '#dtEnd' '10/08/2026'` read back as '25/06/2026 .. 10/08/2026/08/2026' because the input is masked and appends. S04: `type f6e1208 "Rust"` produced no options while `fill` on the same ref did. S06's CodeMirror writes degraded from replace to append and produced invalid JSON two clicks from being saved as live build config, and a force-click reported `{"focused":"dark \| BODY"}`.

**Root cause.** Two distinct gaps the upstream clustering scattered: no layer reports the active element after an action (separate from which tab is foreground, #2), and no doc carries the per-widget rules that every real authenticated app needs. Each failure yields a plausible wrong value rather than an error.

**Missing capability.** Report the active element and the field's before/after value on every fill/type - the single change that converts this whole class from invisible to obvious - plus a short 'modern form widgets' section in SKILL.md covering masked, React-controlled, chip/tag, multi-box OTP and rich-text inputs.

### A24. There is no login handoff primitive and no rule for when to use the viewer, so agents hand-roll waiters in shell, park pages in prose, and reach for the viewer neither when they should nor only when they should

`high` | layer: `mixed` | 6 sessions with hand-rolled waiters + 7 sessions with a mis-set handoff bar; wall-clock stalls of ~36h, ~12min and ~10min in three separate sessions

**Evidence.** S18 stalled ~36 hours on GitHub sudo mode and wrote its own waiter: `for i in $(seq 1 90); do t=$(playwright-cli --raw eval "document.title"); if [ "$t" != "Confirm access" ]; then echo "page advanced"; exit 0; fi; sleep 4; done`. S36 wrote a site-specific `fetch('/api/retail/user/current/wallet')` probe just to answer 'is this origin still authenticated'. On the bar: S04 hit a 2FA login AND walked up to an irreversible Delete on the user's live resume without ever offering the viewer, and the human intervened out of band ('i just updated the resume on resume tab myself'); S05 never opened or mentioned the viewer in 183 calls, including at the moment it handed the task back; S10 guided the human click-by-click through a professional social network report dialogs from memory, getting labels wrong three times while the human pasted a screenshot at every step. Conversely S08 aborted to the viewer for two password fields it could have typed itself, and the user pushed back: 'read cuttle's skill and check full playwright-cli skill / you should be able to do that yourself!'

**Root cause.** The VNC viewer is cuttle's flagship handoff feature and no verb uses it; SKILL.md markets it but sets no bar, so there is no login-state probe before the wall and no completion signal after it.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle handoff <url> --until <predicate> --timeout` that opens the page, prints the exact viewer link focused on the right tab, notifies the human and returns when the page leaves the sign-in origin; `cuttle auth status <domain>` for the before half; and explicit SKILL.md triggers (real captcha, 2FA, bank step, irreversible action on a live account - not 'typing a password').

### A25. Handoff is one-directional and the viewer is a shared, unrecorded, single-framebuffer room: nothing tells the agent the human acted, and with two seeds up there is no per-seed view of which browser the human is even looking at

`high` | layer: `image-viewer` | 1 session narrated it three times; unmeasurable by construction - this is the mechanism behind blind spot 1, which inflates every success rate in the corpus

**Evidence.** S01, three times: 'i entered <redacted-id> myself, and it just worked... you were overthinking stuff' (after 52 agent calls on that modal), 'i did mouse drag myself, check the value now' (after four failed slider strategies), and 'i entered creds myself' - plus 'did that [screenshot]. check the current page pls', the user explicitly instructing the agent to re-read the page after acting in the viewer. Structurally: `Xvnc :99 -geometry 1920x1080` with openbox and -AlwaysShared, every seed's Chrome mapped onto that one framebuffer and sized from fingerprint.ScreenArgs, so the human who opens 6080 gets whichever window openbox has on top, not the one the agent is driving.

**Root cause.** cuttle sits between the viewer and the browser and could see human-originated input, but exposes no event, no diff and no recording; and one X display serves all seeds with no per-seed viewer URL.

**Missing capability.** A 'page changed by viewer input at T' signal in `cuttle status` and annotated onto the next driver result; a per-seed viewer URL that raises and focuses that seed's window; and SKILL.md guidance to re-read state after asking the human to do one step.

### A26. 'Logins persist across down/up' is not true for the sites that matter and there is no way to ask whether a seed is still authenticated, so every session opens by re-running the risky login flow

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 11 sessions; a fixed 3-5 call tax per session plus the credential risk of #13 and #17 paid repeatedly

**Evidence.** All three a freelance marketplace board checks (S23 Aug 6, S27 Aug 10, S33 Aug 12) opened logged out despite the persistent profile and 3008-cookie state snapshots visible in the daemon log; the agent has internalised it - 'Logged out as expected (their sessions always expire)' - and the user's workflow docs now open with 'ASSUME LOGGED OUT', which means the friction is now paid on every future run while generating no new evidence (blind spot 5). S01 found the server-side variant: 'The one thing that actually was broken: your server session had expired. Clicking Entregar an hour ago would have bounced you to the login page and wiped the draft.' S15 attached into a browser holding a timed-out a consumer banking web app tab and a logged-out a consumer banking app that both look like live logins in a tab list.

**Root cause.** The cookie jar persists; the server-side session does not, and the briefing promise reads stronger than reality. Nothing surfaces per-origin auth state, so the only detector is landing on a sign-in redirect.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle auth status [origin]` reporting which origins have stored cookies, when state was captured and how old it is, plus a SKILL.md line stating that persisted cookies do not mean a live server session.

### A27. Reading a page is expensive and awkward: snapshots run to thousands of lines, land as timestamped YAML in whatever directory the shell is in, and must be found with `ls -t` and mined with rg/sed line windows - so agents only ever see the slice they already suspected mattered

`high` | layer: `cli` | 10 sessions; 592 snapshot calls in the window, most of them followed by shell mining

**Evidence.** S28 reconstructed a 27-name guest list with `f=$(ls -t .playwright-cli/page-*.yml \| head -1) && rg -n -i 'sign in\|approved\|guest' "$f"` then `sed -n '190,352p' "$f" \| rg -o 'Profile picture for ([^"]+)"'`. S30 needed seven shell calls to read one a professional social network profile: snapshot -> ls -t -> rg -> sed -> run-code scroll -> snapshot -> rg. S13 grep-and-sed'd snapshots ~12 times, once getting nothing from `sed -n '150,260p'` with no idea what part of the page it had sliced. S20 read a billing page as `document.body.innerText.split('\n').slice(18,60)`.

**Root cause.** No scoped, token-budgeted read primitive: the a11y snapshot is all-or-nothing, is written to disk by the driver rather than returned, and cuttle owns no artifact location of its own.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle read [--selector] [--region dialog] [--text\|--aria]` returning a context-sized extract inline, and a stable artifact directory outside any git working tree with absolute paths printed.

### A28. Content the agent cannot see is systematically mistaken for content that is not there: virtualized lists, collapsed expanders and cross-origin frames all read as empty with no signal, and no count is ever reconciled

`high` | layer: `mixed` | 4 sessions on virtualization/disclosure, 4 sessions touching frames (11 frame-related calls in the window) - restored from the critics; both classes dissolved entirely in clustering

**Evidence.** Virtualization: S01's BIC column 'could not be filled at all' because the virtualized table held ~8 of 33 rows in the DOM - the actual reason it fell back to offline XML surgery, which the clustering misattributed to humanize latency; S09 wrote three byte-identical 251.9K PNGs of a virtualized message list without noticing; S15 saw a 'Tools 14' badge against six extractable names and burned six calls before finding a 'Show 4 more...' expander. Frames: S08's two frameLocator attempts on the cross-origin reCAPTCHA anchor returned only the waiting line and nothing else; S05's ref prefixes drifted e... / f12e... / f2e... across frames, so refs are not merely stale but invisibly namespaced; S25 got a user-pasted page ending 'this page contains 1 cross-origin iframe(s) that could not be accessed due to browser security policies'; S16 noted screenshots were 'the only tool for cross-origin iframe captchas'.

**Root cause.** Every read primitive returns a window over the data and none of them says so. Snapshots do not span frames, there is no frame enumeration, and nothing reconciles a page's own stated count against what was extracted.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle frames` enumerating frames with origin and reachability, extraction that spans frames or names the frame it stopped at, a scroll-and-collect read that reports 'collected N of M claimed', and a stated rule that an empty result on a list-like region means 'not in DOM', not 'not on page'.

### A29. Every input primitive except click and type is missing, including the ones anti-bot UIs specifically gate on: no drag verb, no dwell, and hover/wheel are undocumented despite being load-bearing

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions with hard blocks; only 14 hover and 3 drag commands in the entire 14-day window against 2749 driver calls - restored from the critics, who noted every input cluster was about clicking

**Evidence.** S15 polled GitHub's Authorize button five times reading `disabled=true`, and only `hover f5e83; mousewheel 0 100; sleep 4` flipped it to false - an interaction-gated control that withholds enablement until it sees human-shaped hover and scroll, which is squarely cuttle's stated territory and appears in no cluster. S01's range slider desynced three ways ('says -25, UI says 0, hidden 0') across four strategies and the human dragged it by hand. S02: 'Click landed off-screen (y=1035 vs 959 viewport)... the coordinate click silently does nothing - calendar closes, value unchanged, no error.'

**Root cause.** The humanizer's rich behavioural modelling (curved Fitts-law motion, log-normal keystrokes) is reachable only through click and type. Drag, hover, wheel and dwell have no verb and no doc line, so the interactions most likely to be watched by an anti-bot system are the ones agents perform least naturally, or not at all.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle drag <from> <to>`, `cuttle hover <sel> [--dwell ms]` and `cuttle scroll` on the same humanized path as click, with auto-scroll-into-view before any coordinate action; plus a SKILL.md note that some controls enable only after human-shaped hover/scroll.

### A30. Unsaved in-page state is destroyed by at least seven independent routes with no marker and no checkpoint - the corpus's most expensive consequence class, dissolved across five clusters

`high` | layer: `daemon` | At least 7 distinct destruction routes across 5 sessions - restored from the critics as a cross-cutting class rather than a symptom of each mechanism

**Evidence.** The routes, each evidenced separately: beforeunload accepted (S01, tax declaration draft rebuilt twice); navigation fired while a dialog was pending (same session, SS Direta wizard lost); forced re-auth after silent server-side session expiry (S01: 'Clicking Entregar an hour ago would have bounced you to the login page and wiped the draft'); a side-lookup performed on the same tab; a concurrent client closing the tab (S05's filled Notion dialog); a browser restart the agent could not attribute (S06: 'your logged-in tabs were dropped when the browser restarted mid-session, not by me'); and a driver session teardown. No cluster proposed the one cross-cutting mitigation.

**Root cause.** cuttle knows when a tab holds unsaved client-side state (it sees the beforeunload handler register) and never says so, and no layer offers a way to checkpoint a partially-filled form before a risky action.

**Missing capability.** Mark tabs holding unsaved state in `cuttle status` and in the warning on any command that would navigate, restart or accept a beforeunload; plus a `cuttle checkpoint` that snapshots form values so a destroyed draft is recoverable rather than re-entered by hand.

### A31. Wrong conclusions about cuttle are written durably into user repos and read back as fact by later sessions - misdiagnosis has a write path

`high` | layer: `daemon` | At least 3 documented instances plus three repos now carrying hand-rolled cuttle playbooks - restored from the critics, who noted the docs cluster treated repo-written playbooks purely as evidence of a vacuum, never as a contamination mechanism

**Evidence.** S02 wrote 'cuttle's Chromium never fires the lazy RSC requests' into the user's playbook and had to retract it: 'That was the whole bug - a background tab, not a cuttle limitation. Let me correct the playbook immediately.' S06 committed a 'stale cache' diagnosis into a marketing journal when the evidence showed a server-side redirect, having been unable to inspect the redirect chain (#14). The mechanism that makes this systematic is #7 (no durable home for cuttle knowledge) combined with the daemon actively falsifying diagnostics - Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled makes hasFocus() return true regardless of real input routing, so S06 published a root cause its own evidence contradicted after cycling five theories over ~10 calls.

**Root cause.** Agents cannot introspect cuttle truthfully, so their beliefs about it are wrong at a demonstrable rate; and because cuttle offers no durable place for per-site or per-tool knowledge, those beliefs land in the user's repo where the next session reads them as ground truth.

**Missing capability.** A truthful `cuttle status --focus` / `whoami-tab` reporting which target actually receives Input and stating that under cuttle hasFocus() and visibilityState do not predict input delivery; plus a cuttle-owned place for per-site recipes so playbooks stop being written into unrelated repos.

### A32. Proxy and egress - half of cuttle's stated purpose - is entirely unobservable: a plain connectOverCDP attach dies on the seed's proxy 407 with an error naming neither proxy nor remedy, and no verb reports exit IP or proxy health

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions, one of which lost an entire probe run; restored from the critics - the proxy/geoip category produced zero clusters despite being a core product feature

**Evidence.** S12's subagent lost all six navigations of a probe run to `net::ERR_INVALID_AUTH_CREDENTIAL` because a plain connectOverCDP attach does not handle the seed's proxy 407: 'The probe hit the documented proxy-auth gotcha (no route handler)'. S03 saw `a link-aggregator site:search` take 55s against a usual 8-9s and wrote 'it could equally be a burned proxy exit', then dropped the question inside a release gate it was about to sign off. And the __default__ seed leak (#37) is an egress failure before it is a memory one: the raw text is 'a real headed Chrome with direct, unproxied egress'.

**Root cause.** cuttle handles proxy-407 transparently over its own CDP path, but an attaching driver that does not install a route handler never benefits, and the resulting Chromium error string mentions neither proxy nor cuttle. Nothing anywhere reports the seed's exit IP, its 407 state, or whether an exit is burned.

**Missing capability.** Exit IP, proxy health and 407 state per seed in `cuttle status`; translation of ERR_INVALID_AUTH_CREDENTIAL into a cuttle error naming proxy auth and the remedy; and a briefing line telling an attaching driver what it must do to inherit proxy auth.

### A33. cuttle ships measurable internal fingerprint incoherences with no self-check, so agents hand-roll stealth probes that themselves lie and publish wrong verdicts

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions; ~75% of production renders geometrically incoherent by one measurement - restored from the critics, who noted the clustering kept only the geometry symptom and treated persona as a reporting problem

**Evidence.** S03 published a wrong stealth verdict before catching itself: `document.fonts.check()` is unpatched and returns true for everything (clark patch catalog row 30, never implemented), so the naive probe passes on any input; the agent rebuilt the test as width-measurement against a `NotARealFont12345` control. Same session: `devicePixelRatio: 2` with `(resolution: 2dppx) = false`; `availTop: 0` with 95px removed from the bottom - a Windows taskbar layout under a macOS UA (glim-sh/cuttle#45). S12 measured the geometry end to end: 'innerWidth (1920, hardcoded by cloakbrowser's DEFAULT_VIEWPORT and again by scrapling) exceeds both outerWidth and screen.width on every render... macOS is incoherent on 5/5 of cuttle's screen pool, Windows on 3/4 - so prod is quietly incoherent ~75% of the time too.' S03 measured the attach-side mirror: 'outer=2560x1392 inner=780x403', and found http.go:196 prefixes every unknown query param as `--fingerprint-<key>=` so `--window-size` can never be passed. Sharpest instance of #7: the correct font-probing method had been discovered in a July session and existed only in a transcript.

**Root cause.** Three different numbers (spoofed screen, OS window, driver viewport) are set by three layers that cannot see each other, several patches in the catalog are unimplemented, and cuttle neither clamps, warns, nor self-checks - so the only instrument is an agent-written probe that shares the same blind spots.

**Missing capability.** A `cuttle verify` self-check asserting internal coherence (fonts.check against a control, dPR vs dppx, availTop/availHeight vs persona, screen vs window vs viewport) with a named pass/fail per row; detect and clamp or loudly warn when a client's viewport override contradicts the seed's spoofed screen; and report window/viewport/framebuffer geometry in `cuttle status`.

### A34. Second-channel legs - email and SMS confirmation codes - are a routine part of every authenticated flow in this corpus and have no path, so codes enter the transcript or the flow silently blocks

`medium` | layer: `mixed` | 4 sessions; restored from the critics - no cluster covered the second channel itself

**Evidence.** S11 scraped a a webmail client OTP by `rg`-ing a driver-written snapshot YAML off disk, then typed it with `keyboard.type`. S14 put an SMS code in the transcript because there is no hand-in path (see #17). S08 kept clicking through a wizard while an email confirmation blocked it, until the human said 'you had to confirm the email, i just did that'. S22 could only prove a submission via a a webmail client receipt after the alertdialog self-closed.

**Root cause.** cuttle treats the browser as the whole world, but every real login and most real submissions have an out-of-band leg. There is no way to wait on it, no way to hand a code in without it entering the transcript, and no way to notice that a flow is blocked on one.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle secret prompt NAME` for a human to hand in a code out of transcript (shared with #17), a documented recipe for the mail-check leg, and detection of 'this flow is waiting on an out-of-band confirmation' so it becomes a handoff trigger (#24) rather than a silent stall.

### A35. cuttle has no machine-readable self-description and no instance identity, so a green port probe proves nothing about which cuttle - or whether it is cuttle at all - the driver is talking to, and there is no readiness gate

`high` | layer: `cli` | 4-6 infra sessions; ~60 calls and ~10 minutes of cold-start sleeping in one session alone (S03: 413 Bash calls, zero `cuttle up`/`down`/`open`, 27 cold-start sleeps totalling 602s)

**Evidence.** S12: 'A local cuttle container still on 24022 makes the port-forward's until loop spin unable to bind while the probe goes green off the local container - so --remote-browser silently runs against the local arm64/macOS farm believing it's on cluster amd64/Windows'; the fix was asserting on the persona string in the returned UA. It also had to diff local vs prod by hand: arm64/macOS vs amd64/Windows, /dev/shm 3GB vs 2Gi, --idle-timeout 90 vs 600. S32 had `ab` aliased to `agent-browser --cdp 9333` while cuttle served 9222 - any habitual use would silently drive a different browser with no error. S16's `cuttle up --image ...:0.11.3` printed the CLI's version 0.11.4 on the ready line during an A/B whose entire point was which daemon build was running. S44 wrote `docker compose up -d ... && sleep 12 && curl /json/version` by hand, then diagnosed itself: 'docker compose up -d returns when containers are created, not when they're serving', and the human's correction was blunt: 'it feels like you keep falling back to docker compose directly in most occasions' - the cost being a skipped `cuttle-smoke`.

**Root cause.** Port reachability is treated as identity, nothing stamps which instance and seed a driver session is on, `cuttle status` reports neither the daemon's own version nor its effective configuration, and no verb blocks until the first Chrome is attachable or exits non-zero until it is.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle up --wait` and `cuttle status --json` with a ready boolean, non-zero exit until ready, daemon version, instance id, arch, persona, start time, effective flags (humanize, idle-timeout, shm), per-seed window/viewport, CDP address and bind scope - one command two instances can be diffed with and any consumer can assert against.

### A36. cuttle's CDP surface is unauthenticated with no bind restriction and was reachable by unauthenticated callers on the deployment network

`critical` | layer: `daemon` | 1 session, but the exposure had been live indefinitely and was found only because an unrelated SSRF audit went looking

**Evidence.** S44, verified in a deployed environment: `glim_web_fetch("http://<deployment-host>:9222/json/version")` -> 200, returning the cuttle webSocketDebuggerUrl. The agent's commit note: 'CDP's HTTP endpoints (/json/new, /json/list) manipulate the browser farm.'

**Root cause.** No token, no allowlist, no bind-scope default, and no way for an operator to see which interface the CDP port is bound to.

**Missing capability.** A shared-secret/bearer token on the CDP HTTP+WS surface, loopback-only binding by default with an explicit opt-in to listen wider, and bind scope reported in `cuttle status`.

### A37. A plain discovery GET to /json/version launches the reserved __default__ seed - a real headed Chrome with direct unproxied egress - which is never reaped because no CDP connection arms the idle reaper

`high` | layer: `daemon` | 1 session, but it is a permanent resource and egress leak per probing consumer, and it was landing on the canary that had just been tuned to mirror prod memory exactly

**Evidence.** S12, review finding then confirmed live: 'describeFarm() sends no ?fingerprint=, so cuttle launches the reserved __default__ seed - a real headed Chrome with direct, unproxied egress - and because a discovery-only GET never opens a CDP connection, the idle reaper is never armed, so it is never reaped. Confirmed live: /data/__default__ is running in the local container right now.'

**Root cause.** Metadata discovery and browser provisioning share an endpoint, and reaper arming is tied to CDP connect rather than to spawn.

**Missing capability.** Serve /json/version metadata without spawning a seed (or arm the reaper on spawn, or refuse __default__ for discovery), plus a cheap describe-farm endpoint that costs no browser - and note the egress half belongs with #32, not with memory accounting.

### A38. Driver ergonomics have no contract: output is double-JSON-encoded and mixed with progress chatter, inline JS cannot survive shell quoting, scratch artifacts land in the user's git tree, and verb discovery happens by trial and error mid-task

`medium` | layer: `mixed` | 11 sessions on verb discovery, 9 on quoting (~30 throwaway .js files), 8 on output parsing, 6 on scratch placement - individually small, collectively a multiplier on every session

**Evidence.** Output: S29 hit `jq: error (at board.json:1): Cannot index string with string ("data")` on a raw body of `"{\"data\":[{\"mid\":..."`, re-applied the `jq -r .` fix to four separate fetches, then wrote it into CLAUDE.md as gotcha #1 noting 'Nothing in CLAUDE.md warns about it'; S07 lost two calls to JSONDecodeError because even --raw output carries ANSI-dim '- fonts loaded' lines and an appended 'Shell cwd was reset'. Quoting: S06 wrote 21 of its 40 run-code calls to a file because nested quoting could not survive `page.locator('button:has(use[href=\"...\"])')`; S22's heredoc failed with a misleading `SyntaxError: Unexpected token ')'`. Scratch: S02 swept ~3,100 lines of console logs and page snapshots into a git commit; S18 was hard-blocked by a stale zero-byte `.playwright-cli` file (`Error: EEXIST ... mkdir '~/Projects/pond/.playwright-cli'`) after the failing command had already opened a tab; S05 prefixed all 183 calls with `cd <scratchpad> &&`. Discovery: three different wrong tab verbs in three sessions (`new-tab` S23, `tabs` S26, `navigate` S27), each answered with a 40-line usage block and no 'did you mean'; S04 interleaved `playwright-cli --help \| grep -iE "^ (goto\|open\|nav\|tab)"` with real work on a live account. Also here: per-invocation process startup dominates discrete loops (S16 measured attach 498ms, tab-list 235ms, 10 Hacker News rows in 33ms batched vs ~797ms per discrete round trip), and the two obvious efficiency moves are mutually incompatible because batched refs die on mutations the earlier steps caused (S21).

**Root cause.** cuttle delegates the entire agent-facing surface to third-party CLIs and documents none of their contracts: no stated output shape per verb, no artifact location of its own, no inlined verb cheat sheet, and no statement that run-code is not atomic.

**Missing capability.** A thin `cuttle exec` / `cuttle eval --file --json` emitting only payload on stdout and diagnostics on stderr; a cuttle-owned per-session artifact directory outside any git tree with absolute paths printed; and a `cuttle up` briefing that inlines the 6-8 verbs every session needs per driver plus the batching rule and its counter-rule.

### A39. Lifecycle and compatibility changes are silent: `cuttle up` restarts and discards the tab set with no reason, --recreate's effect on the profile is ambiguous, a bare navigation timeout carries no forensics, removed entrypoints and env vars fail with no message, and CUTTLE_PORT collides with Kubernetes service-link injection

`medium` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions on lifecycle, 3 on timeout forensics, 1 each on the shim and the k8s collision - plus a ~4-minute production outage

**Evidence.** Lifecycle: S40's `cuttle up` returned 'cuttle restarted' and the browser came back with only about:blank - the same command printed 'already running' in two other sessions - with no reason and no warning that the human's logged-in tabs were gone; S07's agent fell back to `docker volume ls` and `docker inspect` because 'the status text from the older image says it discards the profile'; S06 could not attribute tab loss at all. Also here, a benign-but-corrosive one: `playwright-cli attach` routinely prints session-created and `Error: Target page, context or browser has been closed` together (S06's first browser call, and S25 identically), apparently binding to cuttle's immortal keep-alive about:blank, costing ~8 calls of defensive re-attaching. Forensics: S44's 'Two 25s attempts -> browser tier timed out x2 -> 51s -> outcome: timeout' with Loki showing a cloud document suite at 5 attempts / 0 successes over 30 days, one of which was a user whose third try was `/export?format=txt` - 'which also failed... because a browser navigating to a text/plain download produces nothing'; S03 reported then retracted an improvement because a short-video platform flipped pass/fail across runs; S12's subagent: 'A genuine silent challenge would look identical.' Compatibility: S03 found '/usr/local/bin/cuttleserve shim is gone... CLOAKBROWSER_BINARY_PATH is no longer read' with three call sites that would not start; S12 found 'naming the Service cuttle makes k8s inject CUTTLE_PORT=tcp://<cluster-ip>:9222, which collides with cuttle's own --port config var' - CrashLoopBackOff, browser farm down, and 'helmfile template could never catch this; it's a runtime injection'.

**Root cause.** cuttle reports outcomes without causes across its whole operator surface: restarts without reasons, timeouts without evidence, removed contracts without diagnostics, and a config namespace (CUTTLE_*) that is exactly the namespace Kubernetes reserves for service links.

**Missing capability.** State the restart reason and warn that the tab set was discarded; make --recreate print which profile volume it re-attaches; attach cheap forensics to any navigation timeout (last committed URL, title, bytes extracted, content-type, challenge-sentinel match) with a caller-settable per-attempt budget; a one-release deprecation shim that starts and names the change; reject a CUTTLE_PORT that looks like `tcp://host:port` and say so at startup; and hide or mark the keep-alive target so drivers stop reporting a benign error on attach.

### A40. The container boundary and the local-vs-production divergence are undocumented, so the workflow cuttle should most obviously own - verify my local UI change in a real browser - silently routes around it

`medium` | layer: `image-viewer` | 4 sessions on the host boundary, 3 on arch divergence; one session drove a browser 27 times against http://localhost:5173 and never touched cuttle at all

**Evidence.** S02 first opened `http://host.docker.internal:4321/`, flip-flopped to the LAN address `http://<lan-ip>:4321/`, then killed the user's dev server and restarted it with `pnpm dev --host` purely so the containerized browser could reach it, hardcoding a machine-specific IP into a dozen subsequent calls. S19 drove a browser 27 times against localhost:5173 with cuttle untouched. On divergence, S03: a large e-commerce site 'exceeds page.goto's 45s timeout before it ever gets to the anti-bot check', a short-video platform/a large social network 'blow the web tier's 25s render budget' under qemu - and the human, not the agent, unblocked it ('can you do run on ssh bl for example then to check?'), with native measurement showing 'identity:us took 2.5s on bl vs 9.2s locally'; the gate meant to catch this was itself misconfigured ('the canary was provisioned at half of prod - 3Gi vs 6Gi memory, and a disk-backed emptyDir for /dev/shm where prod has a 2Gi tmpfs'), and S12 later found a 'canary-only, blame qemu' comment had been covering a permanent a large social network failure on production-identical amd64 for months.

**Root cause.** cuttle runs the browser in Docker on a multi-arch image where the arch selects the stealth persona and the render budget is a fixed constant, and says nothing about the network boundary, the filesystem boundary, or the fact that a local failure is indistinguishable from a real one.

**Missing capability.** A documented stable in-container hostname for the host (or `cuttle open --host-port 4321` setting up the forward) plus a line stating host paths are not visible to the browser; a loud startup warning when running translated ('render budgets are not meaningful here'); the native-arm64 image named as the local-dev path; and a per-invocation render budget.

### A41. Irreversible outward actions from a logged-in account have no per-action gate, and anti-bot challenge type cannot be identified so agents fight the wrong challenge

`medium` | layer: `skill-docs` | 3 sessions on destructive actions (producing the corpus's sharpest user reaction), 1 on challenge detection

**Evidence.** S04 clicked Delete and then 'Yes, delete' on the user's live resume with only a local backup as mitigation; the user's next message was 'i just updated the resume on resume tab myself'. S12 produced the corpus's sharpest reaction - 'stop stop stop! no issue creation anywhere! what are you doing! i never allowed that!' - while another session's user said 'submit all of them' about equivalent form submits, so the tolerance varies sharply and cannot be inferred. On challenges, S08 ran `mousemove 1392 636; mousedown; mouseup; sleep 8` returning `{"tokenLen":0,"challengeVisible":false}`, plus a 12-iteration polling loop and a `sleep 25`, before concluding 'That's the invisible reCAPTCHA badge, not a checkbox - it mints the token on submit. So nothing to click.'

**Root cause.** Plan-level agreement is treated as authorization for each outward act, and nothing marks destructive affordances on a live account as requiring fresh confirmation. Separately, no verb or recipe reports which challenge is present or whether it needs interaction, and cross-origin frame timeouts (#28) say nothing useful.

**Missing capability.** SKILL.md names destructive affordances on live accounts as a handoff trigger (park the page, print the viewer URL, ask), any cuttle verb performing an irreversible outward action requires per-action confirmation, and `cuttle challenge detect` (or a documented recipe) reports challenge type and whether interaction is required - paired with the scaled element capture from #10 so an image captcha is legible in one call.

### A42. cuttle is not reached for when it should be: agents answer from stale cached fetches on pages the user has a live login for, and the human has to say the word 'cuttle' before a browser is considered

`high` | layer: `skill-docs` | 5 sessions; one confidently-wrong research answer and one feature shipped without ever being verified against the real dashboard

**Evidence.** S30 researched a logged-in-only a professional social network profile from a stale cached public snapshot and reported it as current; the user pushed twice - 'Are you sure that you checked the correct profile?' then 'check hher profile with cuttle' - before the agent ran `which cuttle`. S25's 'try getting those notion pages content through cuttle' was sent only after glim_web_fetch timed out on both URLs. S32 needed three discovery calls after the human said 'could you also use cuttle'. A separate session hit a hard login wall ('Privy needs an emailed code'), abandoned the real dashboard, verified against a throwaway stub route, shipped, and told the user 'One thing left for you, since I can't log in' - having never loaded cuttle at all.

**Root cause.** Skill triggering keys on the word 'browser' rather than on the intents that actually need cuttle: read this page behind a login; a fetch returned a JS shell, a login wall, or a timeout.

**Missing capability.** Trigger the skill on read/open-this-page intents, state the escalation rule explicitly in the briefing (any fetch returning a JS shell, a login wall or a timeout is a cuttle job), and have `cuttle status` list which origins the profile currently holds sessions for so the answer to 'do I have a login for this' costs one call.

### A43. Recurring authenticated jobs have no durable home, so per-site knowledge is re-derived every run and users end up demanding hand-written recipes in their own repos

`high` | layer: `cli` | 6 sessions; measured payoff of codification is 24 calls / 11.6 min -> 2 calls / 47s for the same job

**Evidence.** S29's user: 'why each time it takes so much time for you to just fetch data and compare it with ... could you update pjs/career/CLAUDE.md so that when i ask you to check for new missions on a freelance marketplace - it would be the straightforward workflow for you without erroring on each step?' - and the agent's five listed causes were four browser/driver frictions. S16 quantified it: 'codifying the a freelance marketplace check into 4 commands + a baseline TSV took a recurring job from 24 calls / 11.6 min to 2 calls / 47s.' Two other sessions wrote a consumer banking web app and a professional social network recipes only after paying full price. This is the same vacuum as #7 and the write path in #31, but the cost here is recurring rather than one-off, in a corpus dominated by recurring authenticated checks.

**Root cause.** cuttle has a `context` concept but nothing carries a per-site playbook alongside a seed, so the discovery cost of every authenticated dashboard is paid on every visit by every session.

**Missing capability.** Per-seed/per-site recipes attached to a cuttle context - at minimum a documented convention and location - so a working sequence becomes a durable asset instead of a one-off discovery, and so recipes stop landing in unrelated user repos where they contaminate later sessions.

### A44. Getting a file out of the browser is one-directional and brittle: `cuttle downloads` needs an exact filename with no --latest or glob, and a resource the site renders inline has no capture path

`medium` | layer: `cli` | 3 sessions; ~50 calls in the one session that leaned on downloads as its verification channel

**Evidence.** S01 tracked Chrome's dedup counter by hand across the session - `(3).xml` through `(9).xml` - listing the download directory whenever it lost count; when a PDF opened in a viewer tab instead of downloading, `cuttle downloads` reported nothing and the agent wrote `page.evaluate('async () => { const r = await fetch(...); const b = await r.blob(); const a = document.createElement(...)')` to synthesize a download it could then retrieve. S02 needed three navigations to surface a full-res image URL it could then fetch.

**Root cause.** The downloads surface serves only files Chrome happened to write, addressed by exact name; authenticated resources rendered in-page have no capture path.

**Missing capability.** `cuttle downloads --latest` with glob support, and `cuttle grab <url> [dest]` performing an in-profile authenticated fetch that writes bytes host-side (the natural sibling of #9's `cuttle fetch`).

### A45. `Target.createBrowserContext` is blocked with a message that names no escape hatch, truncates before its remedy, and addresses an audience that cannot act on it

`medium` | layer: `daemon` | 3 sessions; ~15 diagnosis calls plus a full release cycle (implement, review, 319-test gate, image build, canary) and one production browser tier down

**Evidence.** S03: the failure reached the client as a truncated `502 ... {"error": "Browser.new_context: Protocol error (Target.createBrowserContext): Target.createBrowserContext is blocked by cuttle: one identity per seed - attach to the existing default context, or start` - cut off mid-sentence, six times. 'There is no opt-out flag or env var - I grepped for one.' The remedy is addressed to whoever wrote the CDP call, but 'scrapling inside scraping-py calls Browser.new_context internally. That is third-party code we don't control.' The user redirected the whole task: 'lets instead of changing anything on the side of this project - go and make ~/pj/cuttle/ fully compatible first'. S16 hit the same wall later and had to `cuttle up --help \| rg -i allow-context` to find the flag.

**Root cause.** A protocol-level block shipped without a documented opt-out, with a remedy sentence aimed at the wrong audience and long enough to be truncated by every error-wrapping layer above it. Note the block also interacts with #15: newContext is exactly where state capture cannot reach.

**Missing capability.** Lead the rejection with the actionable token in the first 80 characters ('set --allow-context-creation / CUTTLE_ALLOW_CONTEXT_CREATION=1 if your driver cannot attach'), and a compatibility section in SKILL.md listing which stacks call newContext internally.

### A46. The humanizer's own pre-click probes ran in the main world at up to ~17 evaluates per click - the anti-detection feature was itself detectable

`medium` | layer: `daemon` | 1 session found it; fixed in 0.11.4 (verified in source: Page.createIsolatedWorld at humanize.go:1036). Ranked low only because it is fixed - its historical cost is unmeasurable and lands in blind spot 3

**Evidence.** S16: humanize.go:693 sent Runtime.evaluate with no contextId. Measured on live containers: 0.11.3 leaked 2 observable main-world probes per click, 0.11.4 leaks 0. The upstream project had already shipped a release for the same class: 'Fix humanize=True triggering invisible CAPTCHA challenges during clicks. Humanized pre-click checks are now stealth-safe.' For the entire life of 0.11.3 the cost would have landed in transcripts as 'a captcha appeared and the agent fought it', filed against the site.

**Root cause.** Pre-click state probes were issued into the page's own JS context, observable to any page watching for injected evaluation.

**Missing capability.** Fixed. The durable gap is the regression guard: a test asserting zero main-world evaluates per humanized action, and a standing rule that any daemon-side probing of the page uses an isolated world by construction.

### A47. Two operator-facing footguns with no guard: WebAuthn/passkey support is undocumented so the auth method most accounts are moving to gets routed around, and nothing prevents a second cuttle landing on the same host or names the per-seed memory cost

`low` | layer: `mixed` | 1 session each; one ~36h stall and one historical node OOM

**Evidence.** S18: 'I picked GitHub Mobile over the passkey (the container browser reports only partial passkey support) and over the authenticator app (that would mean you reading a TOTP code into the transcript)' - the resulting handoff stalled the whole workstream ~36 hours, and both rejected options are cuttle gaps (#13/#17 for the TOTP half). S44 had to encode a do-not-do-this into a handoff plan: never rename the helm release because 'helmfile won't uninstall a vanished release and you'd end up with a second 6Gi farm on the ~6.9Gi browser node (the 2026-06-12 OOM)'. A separate local investigation measured the cuttle container at 26-32% of a host core - roughly two-thirds of all OrbStack CPU - during unrelated work.

**Root cause.** WebAuthn status in cuttle's Chromium is undocumented so agents probe it and route around it; per-seed memory cost is undocumented and nothing detects a second cuttle already holding the profile dir or port.

**Missing capability.** Document WebAuthn status explicitly in SKILL.md and consider a virtual-authenticator or host-passkey bridge for the handoff path; document per-seed memory cost in the briefing and have the daemon refuse or loudly warn when another cuttle already holds the profile dir or port.
