### clark-labs-inc/clark-browser

Nothing new shipped as a binary since cuttle's fork point: the latest GitHub release is still chromium-v148.0.7778.96-stealth5 (2026-06-01), and the local clone is a shallow graft at df1afb6 (2026-06-22) with no branches or tags beyond it. The entire post-stealth5 delta is 10 unreleased files on main: two new patches (0027-analyser-node-noise, 0047-suppress-cdc-globals), a Python-launcher pass that pins seed-derived screen size + derives the Playwright viewport from it and auto-enables proxy-coherent WebRTC, plus a local JA3/JA4 ClientHello capture harness and its unit tests. The project's direction is entirely engine-side stealth plus validation harnesses; it deliberately owns none of the agent-interaction surface (its only CLI verbs are info/fetch/clear, and its agent story is "use vercel agent-browser over CDP"), so it offers nothing on cuttle's dialogs/overlays, downloads, screenshots, or secret-typing pain. Of the two new patches, 0027 is a genuine gap-closer worth cherry-picking with a correction, and 0047 is dead code in cuttle's headed container and would introduce a louder tell than it hides.

### vercel-labs/agent-browser

Between afae698 (2026-07-08) and 548b159 / v0.34.0 (2026-08-10) agent-browser shipped 32 commits across v0.31.2-v0.34.0, and the dominant theme is exactly cuttle's architecture: several agent sessions sharing ONE Chrome over one CDP port. `--pin-tab` (861e76d) gives each session a persisted session-to-target binding with a stable `tab_gone` error, and event-discovered targets can no longer steal another session's active tab. The second theme is "a tab that cannot answer" - a Memory-Saver-discarded renderer versus a renderer paused by a JavaScript dialog are now distinguished by a bounded liveness probe (`ensure_renderer_alive`), recovered with `Target.activateTarget`, and surfaced as `revived` / `activeTabRevived` / `dialogBlocked` instead of hanging the daemon; that plus the pre-existing pending-dialog gate (fail fast with instructions, and stamp a `warning` onto every response while a dialog is open) is a complete answer to cuttle's pain point 4, which cuttle currently has zero code for - `grep -i dialog internal/serve/*.go internal/cli/SKILL.md` returns nothing. Third, the stream server was rebuilt for human-in-the-loop viewing: split reader/writer tasks, latest-frame-wins, ...

### microsoft/playwright-cli

The git delta since 793cfb3 is tiny - 5 commits, only two functional: a Playwright roll 1.62.0-alpha-<redacted-id> -> 1.63.0-alpha-2026-08-05 (40783a0) and two fixes to the installed-skill freshness check (eee5a18, 4f9d85a). The repo itself is a ~200-line wrapper; the real surface lives in the pinned `playwright-core` bundle, so I diffed the two tarballs directly: same 88 CLI verbs, with `upload <files...>` going variadic, `screenshot --type png\|jpeg\|webp`, `install --global/-g` for skills, and enum hints added to several flag docs. The direction is consolidation, not expansion: three bundled skills now ship inside playwright-core (playwright-cli, playwright-trace, playwright-component-testing), the skill file is treated as generated-from-the-tool (scripts/update.js, .claude/skills/dev/roll.md), and the CLI now nags once a day when the installed SKILL.md drifts from the bundled one. The high-value findings for cuttle are therefore not in the commit range but in the standing architecture - the modal-state gate, the secrets/redaction pipeline, the per-response Page/Modal/Events digest, and the `show` dashboard with annotation handoff - all of which map directly onto cuttle's four ...

### clearcotelabs/clearcote-browser (HEAD da07119, 2026-08-16, SDK 0.27.0; open patch series targets Chromium 149.0.7827.114, 150/151 are licensed "Pro" builds ...

In the last ~30 commits clearcote moved from "a patched Chromium + a Playwright drop-in" to a full agent product: a 20-tool MCP server over one shared stealth browser, a `clearcote-serve` raw-CDP mode that hands the SAME browser to any driver, an in-engine AI agent exposed as a new CDP command `Browser.agentRunTask` (with a record/replay/heal flow cache), an in-engine `Browser.humanizedClick`/`humanizedClickSelector`, and a three-language humanizer with a per-seed motor persona plus an offline detector-emulator that scores it. The stealth work went from "spoof strings" to "prove internal coherence": a self-referential coherence gate with a REQUIRED/KNOWN_GAP contract, launch-time coherence warnings with actionable text, a measured headless window-geometry model (inner<=outer<=avail<=screen), install-integrity manifests, and a documented conclusion that a synthetic seed persona loses to an imported real-machine profile (13/13 fail vs pass on a strict device check). Container work is the closest mirror of cuttle: docker/serve.py launches chrome directly headful on Xvfb and patches exactly the Linux-host-vs-Windows-persona contradictions (Web Bluetooth, Widevine CDM, ANGLE shader ...

## Appendix G - The adversarial critics, verbatim

Two critics attacked the upstream sweep before anything was ranked: one hunting
recommendations that were already solved, would break stealth coherence, or were
cargo-culted from an incompatible architecture; one hunting coverage gaps. Section 6
records their verdicts; this is their reasoning, unedited. The session-side critics are
already reproduced in full as section 7.

### G1. Adversarial critic - what should be cut or reframed

## Themes nobody swept

**WebDriver BiDi is entirely absent, and it is the standards-track answer to theme 1.** Not one of the eight themes mentions `browsingContext.userPromptOpened`, `browsingContext.handleUserPrompt`, `input.setFiles`, `browsingContext.print`, or `session.subscribe`. A researcher reading the BiDi spec and the ChromeDriver/Firefox implementations would have found that the W3C already specified exactly the design all three projects converged on independently - a persistent event subscription that owns prompt state, plus a single verb that clears it - including the parts the sweep got wrong by omission. BiDi answers the question theme 1 leaves hanging (what happens to a prompt raised while nothing is subscribed) with an explicit `userPromptHandler` capability set at session creation, per prompt type, with `accept`/`dismiss`/`ignore` semantics. That is the policy table gap 9 proposes to invent. BiDi also defines `browsingContext.navigationStarted`/`navigationCommitted` as the navigation barrier theme 6 wants to reconstruct by sniffing CDP frames, and it defines file-input setting without a coordinate or a chooser race. Separately, Playwright ships a BiDi backend (`src/server/bidi/`) that the sweep never opened - meaning a whole alternative transport that cuttle's multiplexer would be blind to (it proxies CDP only) went unexamined. If a driver ever attaches over BiDi, every interception in `wsproxy.go` silently no-ops.

**Prompt injection from page content into the agent is not a theme.** It appears once, as a Snyk finding in the playwright-cli community notes, and is never developed. This is arguably the largest omission given cuttle's positioning: the whole product is logged-in sessions on the user's real accounts, and the agent reads page text as input. A sweep of Anthropic/OpenAI computer-use guidance, browser-use's `SecurityWatchdog`, Browserbase/Stagehand's docs, and the 2025-2026 indirect-injection literature would have produced a concrete surface list - snapshot text, console messages, `document.title`, download filenames, and (crucially) the exact channels cuttle itself is proposing to add: overlay descriptions (`div#consent-banner` is page-controlled text), dialog messages (`alert()` content is attacker-controlled and gap 2 proposes to render it verbatim into `cuttle status`), and `Log.entryAdded` frames synthesized by the proxy. Three of the proposed fixes across themes 1, 3 and 6 pipe page-authored strings into the agent's instruction channel with no framing. That is a theme-level finding nobody surfaced.

**Captchas and challenge interstitials are not a theme, only an aside.** Cuttle's stated reason to exist is Cloudflare and human handoff, yet there is no sweep of how anyone detects "we are on a challenge page", waits for it to clear, or decides to escalate to a human. A researcher would have found Browserbase's `solveCaptcha` behavior and its `captchaImageSelector` config, Hyperbrowser's `solveCaptchas` session flag, browser-use's Cloudflare handling, and camoufox's documented Turnstile posture - plus the recurring practical finding that Turnstile clears on a real click inside a *cross-origin iframe*, which cuttle's root-frame-only isolated world (theme 6 gap 3) structurally cannot probe. That single interaction between two unswept facts is a shipping bug.

**Time and clock control is missing from the waiting theme.** No mention of Playwright's `page.clock` or CDP `Emulation.setVirtualTimePolicy`. A researcher would have found that the standard fix for "the settle gate pays 40ms per press and a spinner never quiesces" is to control the clock, not to sample it faster - and would also have found the stealth counter-argument (a page can detect a virtual clock trivially), which is the honest reason cuttle should not use it. Either way the option should have been priced.

**Cost, density and latency were never measured.** Eight themes propose adding a permanently-attached CDP session per seed, `Page.enable` on every page, per-seed Xvnc processes, screencast fan-out, and post-type verification probes, with effort labels but no memory or CPU numbers. Nobody swept how many browsers fit a node, what `/dev/shm` sizing does, what SwiftShader costs per tab, or what the per-turn latency budget actually is. Playwright-cli's maintainer comment ("still within the rounding error of your LLM completion") is the only datapoint in the entire corpus, and it is used rhetorically rather than as a budget.

**Observability of an agent run is absent.** Playwright's trace viewer - `trace.zip` carrying DOM snapshots, network, console, and a timestamped action log - is the single best existing artifact for "what did the agent actually do and where did it go wrong", and it appears nowhere. Neither does HAR recording, rrweb-style session replay, or Browserbase's session inspector/replay. Every theme independently reinvents a slice of this (a ring buffer of gate outcomes, a `/dialogs` history, a `logs` tail) without anyone checking what the mature answer looks like.

**Frames, OOPIFs and shadow DOM are scattered across themes but never swept as one.** Theme 6 notes the root-frame-only probe; theme 3 notes iframe piercing in ref systems; theme 1 notes agent-browser descends same-origin iframes. Nobody assembled the actual constraint: cross-origin iframes are separate targets with separate sessions under flatten mode, `elementFromPoint` cannot cross them at all, coordinates must be translated per frame, and cuttle's humanizer holds one cursor per *connection* rather than per target. Payment forms, consent CMPs, captcha widgets and embedded logins are all in that category - i.e. exactly the hard cases.

## Repos whose most interesting work got skipped

**browser-use.** Only `browser-harness` was read, and only a daemon file plus one skill doc. The main repo's architecture is a set of *watchdogs* - separate observers for downloads, popups, permissions, security, crashes, DOM stability - which is precisely the shape themes 1, 4 and 5 keep proposing piecemeal. A researcher would also have found its DOM serializer (index-based refs with a cached interactive-element scan), its `include_attributes` token-budget knobs, and its published eval harness (WebVoyager/WebArena/Online-Mind2Web runs). That last item is the only source in the corpus that would answer "does any of this actually raise task success rate", a question no theme asks.

**microsoft/playwright itself.** The sweep read the bundled `tools/backend/` CLI layer and a few server files, and read `injectedScript.ts` only through a minified bundle. Never opened: the tracing subsystem, `page.clock`, `route`/`routeFromHAR`, the BiDi backend, `page.pause()`/Inspector (a human-takeover model), and the whole issue tracker - only *playwright-cli's* tracker was mined. Playwright's own tracker is where the multi-year threads on `connectOverCDP` semantics, download behavior over CDP, and `acceptDownloads` live, and it is where the answer to theme 4's `setDownloadBehavior` question is actually settled.

**clark-browser's lineage.** The patch series was read; its *parent* was not. The `--fingerprint-*` switch family originates in adryfish/fingerprint-chromium (ChromiumFish), and ungoogled-chromium is the base for the client-rects/canvas noise that theme 8 wants to remove. A researcher reading those two would have found the original design rationale for the single-global-seed model theme 8 criticizes, the known list of surfaces those switches cover and miss, and - relevant to theme 7 - ChromiumFish's engine-level input work, which clearcote's own RESEARCH.md rates above CloakBrowser's client-side humanizer. That is the direct prior art for theme 7's hardest question and it was cited secondhand rather than read.

**clearcote.** The SDK and patch headers were read closely, but `docs/RESEARCH.md` synthesizes sixteen projects and none of those sixteen were followed. The canvas-bridge design (forward canvas ops to a real-GPU host) was noted in the community pass and never reconciled with theme 8's WebGL gap, where it is decisive: it is the argument that string-spoofing over SwiftShader is unfixable in principle, which changes gap 4's "pick path A or B" into "path A cannot be completed".

**CloakBrowser-Manager.** The RFB relay and clipboard bridge were mined in detail; the actual per-profile Playwright lifecycle, the license/session-cap logic that produced the best silent-failure post-mortem in the corpus, and PR #50's nginx/KasmVNC 1.5 data plane were only read from prose descriptions.

## Sources never consulted

**The CDP spec itself.** No `browser_protocol.json`/`js_protocol.json` sweep, no experimental/deprecated annotations, no version diff. A researcher would have found several things the themes guess at: `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` carries `pointerType`, `force`, `tangentialPressure`, `tiltX/Y`, `twist` - none of which cuttle's humanizer sets, so every synthesized event has a default pressure profile that no real pointer produces, which is a behavioral tell theme 7 never considers. Also `Input.imeSetComposition` (the correct path for the CJK case flagged in the community notes), `Overlay.setInspectMode`/`Overlay.getHighlightObjectForTest` (a browser-side hit test that needs no page evaluate, directly relevant to theme 6 gap 3), `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia`, `Storage` domain, and `Page.addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument`'s `worldName` parameter.

**Chromium source beyond `page_handler.cc`.** One file was read, well. Not read: `render_widget_host_impl.cc` and `web_input_event.h` for the actual `kFromDebugger` propagation path (theme 7 gap 6 asserts a two-line fix without reading the consumers), `synthetic_gesture_controller`, `javascript_dialog_manager` in the headed vs headless split, and `content/browser/renderer_host/input/`. Also no bug-tracker pass - the Chromium tracker carries the authoritative history on tab discarding, `background: true` on desktop (theme 3/community notes call it "undocumented"), and file-chooser interception.

**patchright, rebrowser-patches, nodriver.** All three named in the prompt, none swept. patchright is the *implemented* answer to theme 8's `Runtime.enable` gap - it patches out the leak, the `__playwright__binding__` global, the `addScriptToEvaluateOnNewDocument` isolated-world tell, and the console leak, and it documents what breaks when you do (exactly the `exposeFunction`/`page.on('console')` regressions theme 8 gap 2 reasons about from first principles). rebrowser-patches ships the canonical test page with named checks (`runtimeEnableLeak`, `sourceUrlLeak`, `dummyFn`, `exposeFunctionLeak`, `mainWorldExecution`), which is the ready-made adversarial suite theme 8 gap 8 proposes to assemble. nodriver would have shown a CDP-direct design that never enables Runtime at all, and its dialog/file-input handling.

**camoufox.** Named, not swept. It is the strongest counter-argument in the field to cuttle's whole engine strategy (Firefox/Juggler, fingerprint injection below the JS layer, no CDP at all), and - directly relevant to theme 7 - it ships a documented humanized cursor (Bezier plus a WindMouse-style algorithm) with published parameters. That is a second independent motion model to score against clearcote's, which would have turned theme 7's single-source benchmark into a comparison.

**The commercial session platforms.** Browserbase, Steel, Kernel, Hyperbrowser - four products solving cuttle's exact problem, zero consulted. Steel is open source and is the closest structural analogue to cuttle's container half; a researcher would have found its session API, CDP proxy, file upload/download endpoints, and extension loading. Browserbase's live-view iframe with an interactive/takeover toggle is the shipped version of what theme 3 gap 5 and theme 5 gap 6 propose to build, and its Contexts API is the shipped version of theme 5's profile portability. Their docs also settle the upload question (get bytes into the session first, then hand a session-local path) that theme 4 gap 1 rediscovers.

**Detection research as a body of work.** CreepJS's source, FingerprintJS's research posts, Vastel's blog, and the mouse-dynamics literature are all absent as primary sources; theme 7's entire evidence base is one vendor's self-authored scorer, which its own header calls an approximation. A researcher would have found published feature sets for behavioral scoring (velocity profile, pause distribution, curvature, dwell/flight histograms) and, more usefully, the recurring finding that commercial scorers weight *session-level* aggregates - action cadence, revisit patterns, idle structure - over single-gesture kinematics, which would reorder theme 7's priorities away from the trajectory rewrite and toward the inter-action pacing item it rates lowest.

### G2. Coverage critic - what was never investigated

Reviewed against the actual tree (`~/Projects/cuttle`), not just the sweep's own citations. Verified as I went: `packages/browser/patches/0013-window-outer-and-dpr.patch` hard-returns `outerWidth = clark::seed::Screen().width` and `outerHeight = screen.height - taskbar` with no reference to the real frame; `internal/serve/http.go routes()` is exactly the six handlers claimed; `insertTextMaxRunes = 80` and `interKeyBaseMs = 85` / `keyHoldBaseMs = 24` are as quoted; `internal/cli/SKILL.md` has zero dialog mentions; `--fingerprint-webrtc-ip` appears only in `clark_fingerprint_switches.cc` and `internal/fingerprint/proxy.go`, consumed by no patch; there is no `media_values` patch in the series. So the sweep's factual base is sound. The judgment is where it falls apart.

## Already solved, in cuttle or in the driver it ships

**"cuttle's humanizer happily drives a renderer that is frozen behind a dialog"** and **"cuttle is blind to JavaScript dialogs, and its own settle gate is the thing that pays for it"** are filed as agent-blindness. They are not. The sweep's own state-of-the-art section documents that playwright-core keeps a per-tab `ModalState[]`, races every snapshot and action against it, refuses every tool without `clearsModalState`, prints `- ["alert" dialog with message "x"]: can be handled by dialog-accept or dialog-dismiss` into the snapshot, and exposes `playwright-cli dialog-accept` / `dialog-dismiss`. On the default driver the agent is told, in band, on every action. What cuttle uniquely owns is the narrow window the sweep buries at the end of gap 1: a dialog raised while no driver is attached is never replayed on the next `Page.enable`, so the tab wedges permanently. That is real and only the daemon can fix it. It is maybe a fifth of what these gaps claim, and gaps 2, 3 and 8 of theme 1 are largely re-implementing verbs playwright-cli already ships, one layer down.

**"No auto-dismiss policy for the one dialog class that is pure noise"** is worse than redundant, it is self-defeating. Playwright registers a dialog listener on every page, so the daemon and the driver would both call `Page.handleJavaScriptDialog` and the loser gets InvalidParams. The proposed guard is a `bytes.Contains("Page.enable")` prefilter setting `driverHandlesDialogs`. Every driver sends `Page.enable` on every page. The flag is true essentially always, so the auto-dismiss is dead code by construction, and in the rare case it fires it is racing the driver it was designed to defer to.

**"There is no way to capture a seed's page when no driver is attached"** (the `Page.getLayoutMetrics` + `captureBeyondViewport` endpoint) is playwright-cli's `screenshot` verb rebuilt inside the multiplexer. It also cannot see the thing that motivated the whole sweep. Three separate gaps across themes 1 and 3 propose screenshots; only the framebuffer grab is a real capability, and the CDP one should be dropped rather than shipped alongside it.

**"No aggregated view of what exists across seeds"** and **"No session-inspection verb"** overstate the build. `chromePool.status()` already returns pid/port/seed/connections/timezone/locale/proxy on `GET /`, and `runningSeeds` in `internal/cli/commands.go` already fetches it and discards everything but auth state. That is a display fix, not a new endpoint family.

**"No handling for renderer-discarded targets"** concedes in its own risk field that cuttle's per-page `Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled` pin makes discards unlikely. The only concrete change it then asks for is folding discard-disabling into `--disable-features`, which the gap itself warns will silently drop `NoReferrers,NoCrossOriginReferrers,MinimalReferrers` if done naively. Net expected value is negative.

## Would break stealth coherence

**"No post-launch geometry verification anywhere"** is the worst recommendation in the sweep, specifically the second half: "add a one-shot fit in `internal/serve/pool.go`: `Browser.getWindowForTarget` -> `Browser.setContentsSize`". Patch 0013 returns `outerWidth`/`outerHeight` as constants derived from the spoofed screen. They do not track the real window. Clearcote's `fitPlan()` converges because in their engine `outer*` is real, so measuring the shortfall and adding it back closes the gap. Ported onto cuttle it re-reads a constant: the measured shortfall never changes no matter how many times you resize, so the loop either no-ops or walks `window.inner*` away from any value the persona explains while `outer*` and `screen.*` sit still. That is the exact incoherence `ScreenArgs` and `dropMaximizeIfSized` exist to prevent, arrived at by the front door.

The same kill lands on **"Nothing stops a driver from destroying the geometry the fingerprint layer just established"**, which says to "make the rewrite-to-setContentsSize variant the default so the common case (a viewer asking the page to fill its canvas) still works". That is a viewer silently setting `inner*` to the size of a human's browser tab. Block `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` and `Browser.setWindowBounds` with a CDP error, mirroring `--allow-context-creation`. Do not rewrite them into a resize that the spoof cannot follow.

**"The X framebuffer is a fixed 1920x1080..."** contradicts its own theme. Gap 1 says run xrandr "on the FIRST attach for a seed"; gap 8 in the same theme says the resize primitive must run "before Chrome launches". Gap 8 is right. A mid-session RandR resize fires real resize and visualViewport events in a live page, and it makes the framebuffer equal to the window, which is exactly the condition under which the entrypoint's `--start-maximized` starts looking harmless again. `dropMaximizeIfSized` is currently the only thing keeping that flag from stretching `inner*` past the spoofed `outer*`.

**"cuttle has NO secret mechanism of its own"**, part (c): redaction applied to the CDP wire. Byte-replacing a secret inside forwarded frames corrupts base64 `Network.getResponseBody`, `Page.captureScreenshot` payloads and `IO.read` chunks, while simultaneously missing the common case where the value appears JSON-escaped rather than literal. Playwright's `redactSecrets` is safe because it runs on rendered text at three known chokepoints, not on a transport. Ship part (b), the `{{cuttle:NAME}}` sentinel expanded in `handleInsertText`. It is genuinely driver-agnostic and strictly better than playwright's post-hoc scrub, because the literal never enters the call log at all. Drop part (c).

**"No clipboard path exists anywhere in cuttle"**, part (c): "give the humanizer a paste path... the ONLY way to fill fields that reject synthesized keystrokes". A field that receives its entire value with zero keydown/keyup is the `fill()` tell in a new costume, the one CloakBrowser's own README says reCAPTCHA behavioral analysis flags, and the one cuttle's insertText rewrite exists to eliminate. The XTEST variant additionally needs window activation, which on the shared `:99` display yanks focus away from every other seed in the container. Keep the clipboard on the human-handoff leg (parts a, b, d) and delete the automation paste path.

**"Humanization is a single container-lifetime boolean"** proposes a live `off` tier, then mitigates the obvious hazard with "refuse to apply `off` to a session that has already emitted humanized input to the same top-level origin". cuttle cannot implement that mitigation. The proxy tracks no navigation state at all; that is the entire premise of theme 6 gap 1. The safety valve depends on a gap the same sweep says is unbuilt. Ship the speed tiers that stay inside human envelopes and leave `off` at daemon-start only.

**"No pre-goal pointer entropy, and the cursor starts at the origin"**, part (b): a daemon goroutine injecting mouse moves the driver never requested, into a page the agent may be mid-gesture on. cuttle already deleted its off-centre press re-aim because a stray micro-move between approach and press broke Material/CDK selects. This is the same failure with a longer fuse. Part (a), seeding `curX`/`curY` off `(0,0)`, is nearly free and should ship on its own.

## Cargo cult

**"Every seed's Chrome shares one X display (:99)... give each seed its own Xvnc display like the manager does"**. CloakBrowser-Manager is a profile manager: a human looks at one profile at a time, N is small, and there is no headless mode because the viewer *is* the product. cuttle's primary deployment has `CUTTLE_VNC` unset. Spawning N Xvnc processes, N openbox instances and N framebuffers to serve a surface most deployments never open is copying the shape of a different product. The cheap tier inside the same gap, `xdotool windowactivate` on the seed being handed off, is the entire real requirement.

**"No 'what changed since my last action' primitive at any level"** talks itself out of existing. agent-browser has `diff_snapshots` because it owns the snapshot; cuttle owns neither snapshot nor screenshot, and the gap concludes "leave the semantic diff to the drivers". What remains is passing `dedup=1` through one query string. That is a line of code inside another gap, not a gap.

**"Single global --fingerprint seed per persona"** is a straight Brave transplant via clearcote. Per-eTLD+1 farbling exists to stop cross-site tracking of one real human across sites they did not choose to link. cuttle's seeds are disposable synthetic identities whose selling point is that they stay identical across sessions so logins persist. "Two unrelated sites can link a cuttle persona to itself" is not a threat cuttle has. The cost is touching every noise consumer plus a build-failure-grade migration. The gap half-concedes this ("partly a deliberate cuttle design choice") and then files it anyway.

**"Three CDP input surfaces bypass the humanizer entirely"** reaches the right answer in its own risk field and then buries it: log one warning when an unhandled `Input.*` crosses a humanize-enabled session, and build nothing until that warning fires. Writing touch, drag and synthesizeScrollGesture handlers for a desktop-persona proxy that has never observed one of those methods is exactly the "no abstraction for a hypothetical caller" the non-negotiables forbid.

**"cuttle has no notion of which attached client owns which tab"**, rated critical / effort large. agent-browser needs `tab_binding.rs` because its daemon multiplexes *sessions* inside one browser. cuttle multiplexes *seeds*, SKILL.md already documents one driver at a time, and the answer to two agents is two seeds, which is the product. The non-strict half (remember the target and re-select it on reconnect) is defensible and small. The strict half, filtering `Target.getTargets` and `/json/list` per client, is a large build against an invented caller that also hands a future bug a lie to hide behind.

## One cross-theme contradiction worth resolving before any of this ships

Theme 2 says to print `PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_TIMEOUT_ACTION=30000` on the attach line in `internal/cli/drivers.go:59`, calling it "the single highest-leverage line in the repo". Themes 1 and 6 say the top pain is silent hangs on blocked pages and that everything should fail fast. Raising the driver's action timeout sixfold makes every blocked-page failure take 30 seconds to surface, globally, so that long values can be typed. Fix the cap instead: `insertTextMaxRunes = 80` at a measured ~110 to 135ms per char is a 9 to 11 second budget against a verified 5000ms default (`README.md:558`), so the cap is roughly 2x too large and the 38-to-80-rune band times out today. Lower it, and split rather than forward verbatim above it.

## What survives the attack cleanly

The WebRTC no-op (switch declared, zero consumers, and cuttle pays a live proxy round-trip in `ResolveWebRTCArgs` to compute a value nothing reads, while `docs/STEALTH-VERIFICATION.md` claims it works), the missing `MediaValues` patch (no `media_values` file anywhere in the series, and 0013 pins DPR 2.0 for macos, so every arm64 seed reports `devicePixelRatio === 2` with `matchMedia('(resolution: 2dppx)')` false), the AnalyserNode cherry-pick, the SKILL.md "Blocked page" section, the desktop framebuffer grab, and the daemon-side dialog observer scoped strictly to the unattached and re-attach windows. Those six are the sweep.

One caveat on the framebuffer grab: `GET /api/get_screenshot` against the shared `:99` returns every live seed's window in a single image, over the same loopback port that already serves `/downloads`. In a multi-seed container that is a cross-identity leak. Crop to the seed's window via `xdotool getwindowgeometry` on the pool's stored PID. That is the fix, not per-seed Xvnc.

## Appendix H - Cross-session patterns

Observations from the batched readers - each held several transcripts at once, so these
are patterns no single session could reveal. They fed the clustering; preserved here
because the raw observation is often sharper than the cluster it became.

**H1.** Three patterns are only visible across the batch.

(1) Every real driving session converged on the same escape hatch - raw JS via `run-code` / `--raw eval` - and each time it was a workaround for a missing cuttle capability, not a preference. Session 2 used it to fill 1049 chars (because humanized typing would have blocked for minutes), to read billing text (because snapshots were drowned in console chrome), and to verify a textbox was empty (because there is no 'what is on screen' verb). Session 4, auditing the source read-only, independently proved why that is dangerous: cuttle's humanizer intercepts exactly two CDP methods, `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` and `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` (humanize.go:127-130), so anything going through `Runtime.evaluate` bypasses humanization entirely AND produces isTrusted=false. The friction and the stealth regression are the same defect: cuttle makes the safe path slow and gives agents no fast safe alternative, so they route around the whole architecture. A bulk-text/paste verb and a blessed read-extraction path would close both at once.

(2) Session 4 also documents that cuttle's own SKILL.md is factually stale in two places agents rely on: SKILL.md:199-206 claims typing is 'rewritten into human-paced motion' when `fill`/`type` actually go through `Input.insertText`, which the humanizer never sees (`rg -n \"insertText\" cuttle` -> zero hits), and SKILL.md:200 claims 'off-centre clicks' that humanize.go:398-403 explicitly removed. Session 2's agent used `box.fill(txt)` believing it was the humanized path. Docs drift here directly caused a wrong stealth decision in a live session.

(3) Secrets are structurally unprotected on the path agents actually take. Session 4 established that playwright-cli's `PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_SECRETS_FILE` substitution reaches only `type` and form-fill, never `run-code` - the vm sandbox has no `process`, so a secret inside run-code must be written literally into the shell command, landing in argv and the transcript before redaction (which only touches output) can run. Session 2's agent had already adopted run-code as its default fill mechanism for non-secret text; the same reflex on a login form would leak the credential. cuttle has no secret-injection verb of its own, so the only protection is a driver feature that the dominant driving style silently disables.

(4) Both audit sessions (3 and 4) exist at all because someone had to reverse-engineer cuttle's behaviour from source to answer questions an agent should be able to ask the daemon - which persona am I, is this input humanized, what is blocking my click. That is itself a diagnostics gap: two full subagent sessions of pure source reading substituted for introspection cuttle does not expose.

**H2.** Both sessions ran against the SAME cuttle container at the same time on 2026-08-07, and each was the other's invisible saboteur - session 1 saw \"two a SaaS console tabs\" appear and its filled a note-taking app form vanish; session 2 saw \"a note-taking app, a cloud provider, an internal tool tabs\" appear and its document focus stolen mid-edit. Neither could see the other, neither could name it from cuttle output, and both independently invented the same class of workaround (foreground the tab, then assert state before/after every write). That is the strongest signal in the batch: cuttle multiplexes CDP but exposes no notion of who else is attached or which targets are claimed, and the failure mode is always silent - clicks land nowhere, selectAll no-ops, forms are closed out from under the agent, and the error surfaced to the agent blames the selector.\n\nSecond cross-cutting pattern: in both sessions the agent stopped using playwright-cli's own click/fill verbs within the first few minutes and never went back, because actionability checks never settle on animated SPA/consent UIs. Session 1 fell to raw coordinate mouse events; session 2 fell to DOM .click() dispatch. Both are downgrades for a stealth browser (DOM dispatch produces untrusted events; coordinate clicking requires an extra JS round-trip per click). cuttle's own SKILL.md says nothing about this, so each session paid the discovery cost separately.\n\nThird: perception asymmetry. Session 2 took two screenshots and used them productively; session 1 took zero in 184 browser calls and consequently misdiagnosed three failed clicks as a disabled button when the real cause was a backgrounded tab. Nothing in cuttle nudges toward looking at the page - there is no screenshot verb and no guidance - so whether an agent can see is left to chance.\n\nFourth: both sessions had to hand-roll wait-for-condition polling in bash (sleep+goto+cache-busting query params), and one had a foreground sleep rejected by the harness. Neither cuttle nor the driver offers a wait-until primitive, so \"is it stuck or just slow\" was answered by burning 25+ calls in one case and 12 minutes of a frozen remote build in the other.

**H3.** Three patterns only visible across both sessions.

(1) Visual perception is the single weakest link, and in BOTH sessions the human caught the agent's error rather than the agent catching it. Session 2's screenshots timed out on font-loading, so the agent verified via a guessed API field and shipped a profile with eight empty image slots until the user sent a screenshot ("you forgot to add imags for each project"). Session 1 had no screenshot path at all, so the agent wrote an inline `document.fonts.check()` probe, got a false positive on every font, and reported an incorrect stealth verdict before disproving itself. Neither agent ever looked at the page with its own eyes; both substituted a data read that lied.

(2) Both agents' dominant mode was raw eval / raw CDP rather than driver verbs, and cuttle's SKILL.md actively encourages it ("drive the site's own JSON API from an authenticated page context"). Session 2: ~200 of ~250 browser calls were `--raw eval`, including monkey-patching `window.fetch` and `XMLHttpRequest.prototype.send` to learn payload shapes and grepping the app's JS bundles from inside the page to recover an enum. Session 1: 48 raw-CDP commands, including rewriting a repro to use `Target.createTarget` because Playwright's teardown closed its tabs. Every one of these is a missing-capability signal - no network observation verb, no target/tab listing verb, no file injection without a modal, no screenshot that works.

(3) Both sessions ended with the agent declaring success on something that had silently not taken effect, and both times only a read-back caught it. Session 2: a skills PATCH returned 200 and changed nothing. Session 1: a state capture returned `err == nil` with zero cookies, overwrote the good auth snapshot, and the profile dir was deleted behind it; and a canary reported 9/10 green while the daemon had panicked and died. cuttle gives an attached agent no way to ask "are you still alive, and did that actually land" - `cuttle status` reporting daemon uptime/panics and last-capture outcomes would have collapsed hours of work in session 1 and prevented a false green in both.

(4) The VNC human-handoff, cuttle's designed answer to exactly these moments, was never used in either session. Session 2 hit a 2FA login AND an irreversible Delete on the user's live account; the human intervened by driving the browser themselves out-of-band ("i just updated the resume on resume tab myself"). The agent never offered the viewer. Handoff is a capability nobody reached for, which suggests the skill does not name its triggers.

**H4.** Three patterns only visible across both sessions.

1. Every hard failure in both sessions was SILENT, not an error. Session 1: a Continue button that does nothing, with the real constraint (a 35-char address cap) buried in a JS bundle the agent had to fetch and grep. Session 2: `fill <ref> HC_PASS` typing the literal string 'HC_PASS' into a password field with zero warning. In both cases the agent's first hypothesis was wrong ("Area of Interest is the blocker", "the secret path truncates"), and in both cases it took a human turn to unstick it. cuttle's biggest agent-experience gap is not a missing verb - it is that nothing in the stack ever says "that did not do what you think it did". A post-action verdict (cuttle already computes a settle gate and aria-toggle check internally for humanized clicks) and a hard error on unknown secret names would each have saved ~10 turns.

2. The agent reached for the same three workarounds in both sessions, and every one names a missing cuttle capability: raw page-context JS via eval (missing: shadow-DOM-safe state read), Python/PIL image post-processing on screenshots (missing: scaled element capture), and shell-side `| tail -2 | python3 -c json.loads` parsing of driver stdout (missing: a clean machine-readable output contract). Two of those shell pipelines crashed on output drift.

3. Both sessions ended with the human correcting the agent about the TOOLING rather than the task - "have you checked full skills/docs of both tools ... doing some dumb shit instead?" (session 1) and "read cuttle's skill and check full playwright-cli skill / you should be able to do that yourself!" (session 2). In session 1 the agent admitted 12-15 of ~30 browser calls were avoidable; in session 2 it had already loaded the cuttle skill and still missed the attach-time secrets semantics badly enough to abort to the human viewer. The `cuttle up` briefing tells agents which drivers exist and how to attach, but not the handful of operational facts that actually decide success: read the driver's full reference first, locators pierce shadow DOM and eval does not, never force/dispatchEvent through cuttle (it defeats humanized input), the secrets file binds at attach, host file paths are invisible to the containerized browser, and humanized input is slow by design. Both sessions failed on items from that list.

4. File movement is one-directional and it cost real deliverable quality. cuttle serves downloads over HTTP but has no upload path; in session 1 the agent silently skipped the registration form's `lwc_upload_file` field, the human caught it only after submission, and by then the wizard had locked the a national tax id (`attachmentsJSON: ""`), forcing the document out of band by email. Session 2 never needed an upload, so the gap only shows up when you look at both.

**H5.** Four patterns only visible across the batch:

1. ZERO SCREENSHOTS IN THREE CUTTLE SESSIONS. Across ~80 combined browser calls in S17, S18 and S20, not one screenshot was taken. All perception was JSON fetches, accessibility snapshots, or `document.body.innerText` slicing. Meanwhile the humans supplied 13 screenshots ([Image #1]-[Image #12] in the a link-aggregator site session, [Image #10] in the payments session) and in both cases the screenshot was how a defect got caught - the human is functioning as the agent's eyes. cuttle has no screenshot verb and SKILL.md never tells an agent to look at the page, so no agent ever tries.

2. ALL THREE CUTTLE SESSIONS HIT A HUMAN-IN-THE-LOOP AUTH WALL, AND EACH INVENTED A DIFFERENT WAITING PROTOCOL. GitHub sudo mode (S18, ~36h stall, resolved with a hand-written 90-iteration document.title polling loop), a crypto exchange console 2FA (S20, ~10 min stall, resolved by parking a tab and writing prose instructions with the viewer URL), and a link-aggregator site (S17, pre-logged-in, so the agent instead hand-rolled a Bash sleep-timer + PushNotification to coordinate with the human). The viewer at :6080 is cuttle's flagship handoff feature and there is no verb that uses it: no `cuttle handoff`, no login-state probe, no completion signal. Every agent rebuilds it badly out of shell loops.

3. TAB OWNERSHIP IN THE SHARED BROWSER IS THE #1 RECURRING BUG. All three cuttle sessions had an incident: evals landing on the human's a freelance marketplace/a hosting provider tabs and returning `SyntaxError: Unexpected token '<'` (twice), a failed tab-new leaving a duplicate tab behind, a monitor dying because the human closed the tab it polled, and an agent narrating that it "restored" a tab it had navigated away. Tabs are addressed by positional integer in a browser a human freely edits, so every index an agent memorizes goes stale - and two of these sessions had to carry "tab N is HIS, tab M is mine" as standing state through compaction, where it then became wrong.

4. THE DOMINANT REAL USE OF CUTTLE IS AUTHENTICATED DATA EXTRACTION, NOT PAGE AUTOMATION - AND IT HAS NO VERB. S17 spent 24 of 29 calls on in-page `fetch()` of a link-aggregator site .json ("plain curl is bot-blocked"); S20 scraped query ids and 69 result rows off Dune pages because no API lists them, and read a billing page as a text-line array. Only S18 did genuine form interaction. A `cuttle fetch`/`cuttle extract` running in the seed's context would replace the most-used capability in the batch, and would also have kept the a link-aggregator site comment monitor alive when the tab went away.

5. THE SILENT ESCAPE HATCH: session S19 drove a browser 27 times against http://localhost:5173 and never touched cuttle - it used standalone `agent-browser --session-name surf` with its own state file. cuttle runs in a container, so a host dev server on localhost is unreachable from it, and nothing in the tooling flags this or offers a bridge. The result is that the exact workflow cuttle should own (verify your local UI change in a real browser) silently routes around cuttle, and that session's browser state lives in a second, unmanaged profile.

**H6.** Three patterns are only visible with both sessions side by side.

(1) The dominant failure mode in both is BLINDNESS, and both agents compensated the same way: by writing raw JavaScript. Session 1 ran 208 evals against 6 screenshots; session 2 ran 223 eval/run-code calls against 4 clicks. In neither session did the agent trust a driver verb to have worked - session 1 invented a save-the-form-XML-and-grep-it verification loop after every field, session 2 invented MouseEvent dispatch and in-page API fetches. When an agent replaces a tool's entire high-level surface with hand-written JS, the missing capability is post-action verification and a visual ground truth, not more verbs.

(2) Invisible native dialogs cost real state in BOTH sessions, in two different shapes: session 1 lost a completed tax declaration draft to unseen "Leave site?" prompts four times (user: "i'm tired of you getting stuck on 'Leave site?' screens"); session 2 had a dialog held by one driver session deadlock a tab for a second driver, which reported the misleading "tab is not responding". That second case is specifically a multiplexer problem - no single driver should be able to orphan a dialog on a shared browser. Dialog handling is the single highest-value missing daemon feature across both.

(3) In both sessions the human eventually took the keyboard for a specific mechanical act the agent could not perform: typing a a national tax id into a modal (session 1, after 52 failed calls), dragging a slider to -25% (session 1), and in session 2 the agent handed a whole profile-edit task back to the user before discovering the real cause was a background tab. The consistent trigger is a widget whose true state the agent cannot read. Also notable: both sessions ended with the agent writing a private cuttle/driver playbook into the user's personal repo (a 34-line block in pjs/finances/CLAUDE.md, a rewritten tools/a professional social network-access/CLAUDE.md with a "three time-wasters" section). Two independent users' repos now carry hand-rolled cuttle documentation - including, in session 2, a false claim about cuttle that had to be retracted. That is the clearest signal that the embedded SKILL.md and the `cuttle up` briefing are missing the facts agents actually need.

**H7.** Five patterns are only visible across the batch.

1. SILENT ACTIONS ARE THE #1 TAX. In three of four sessions the dominant time sink was the same shape: the agent performed an action and could not tell whether it worked. a hosting provider Submit did nothing (session 1: bot-check 403, found by grepping a console log; session 2: required attachment, found only by screenshotting and reading a tooltip); Superteam Save gave no confirmation so the agent paid a full reload per write batch (session 3); a page vanished with 'Target page, context or browser has been closed' and cuttle's own logs said 'clean disconnect' (session 4). Cuttle already gates clicks on a settle + aria-toggle verify - it is the one component positioned to answer 'the click landed, nothing changed, and one request failed 403'. A single 'what changed after my last action' signal (DOM diff + new console errors + failed requests + new overlays) would have cut the largest cost in this batch.

2. THE ACCESSIBILITY SNAPSHOT IS NOT ENOUGH, AND SCREENSHOTS ARE THE LAST RESORT INSTEAD OF THE FIRST. The blocking information was in pixels twice (a tooltip, a code view scrolled right) and the agent reached for a screenshot only after escalating input mechanisms. Meanwhile screenshots themselves were awkward: wrong flag (--path vs --filename), files landing in whatever cwd the shell drifted to, and three byte-identical captures of a virtualized list before the agent gave up on pixels. Cuttle has no screenshot verb at all, so this is entirely inherited driver behaviour that nothing documents.

3. HUMANIZATION IS FIGHTING THE DRIVER'S TIMEOUTS AND LOSING BOTH WAYS. In session 3 a 56-char `fill` returned a hard TimeoutError on text that had actually been typed - twice, on the user's live profile, where a naive retry would have duplicated it. In session 2 the agent gave up on humanized typing entirely for long bodies and routed around it with `page.keyboard.insertText`. So the fields that most need to look human were filled by the least human path, and the fields that used the human path reported false failures. Neither behaviour is documented anywhere; both agents rediscovered them independently.

4. EVERY SESSION REDISCOVERED THE SAME FOUR DRIVER MECHANICS, AND ONE WROTE THEM INTO A PROJECT DOC INSTEAD. `run-code` is Node not the page (sessions 2 and 3), `run-code` needs an `async page => {}` wrapper (session 3), long text needs insertText (sessions 2 and 3), `click` sometimes needs raw mouse coordinates (sessions 1 and 2). Session 2 ended by writing all of this into `pjs/career/docs/SCAM_REPORTING.md` under a heading 'Driving these forms with cuttle' - a scam-reporting playbook is now carrying cuttle's driver documentation, which means it helps exactly one project. These four belong in cuttle's embedded SKILL.md.

5. IDENTITY AND OWNERSHIP OF A CUTTLE INSTANCE / SEED / TAB IS UNDERSPECIFIED AT EVERY SCALE. Session 2: the agent and the human contended for the single visible viewport, and the agent degraded its own evidence capture to avoid stealing focus, negotiating by prose ('don't fight me for the tab'). Session 3: careful manual bookkeeping of tab indices to avoid touching the user's tabs 0 and 1. Session 4: local dev and production computed the same deterministic seed and landed on the same single-replica Chrome, where either side's `browser.close()` kills the other's page; and a green TCP probe on port 24022 proved nothing about which cuttle instance was behind it - the agent ended up asserting on the returned UA string as a proxy for instance identity. A stable instance identity on the HTTP surface, per-client seed namespacing, and a documented multi-consumer contract would address all three.

One non-browser pattern worth flagging because it recurs: in sessions 1, 2 and 4 the agent staged outward-facing actions (form submits, GitHub issues) and the human's tolerance for them varied sharply - session 1 said 'submit all of them', session 4 produced 'stop stop stop! no issue creation anywhere! what are you doing! i never allowed that!'. Anything cuttle adds that performs an irreversible outward action from a logged-in account inherits that ambiguity and should require per-action confirmation rather than a plan-level agreement.

**H8.** Five of the six sessions never touched a browser at all, and the one that did (S44) used cuttle purely as infrastructure - starting the container, curling its CDP /json/version, inspecting its image architecture - never as a page-driving tool. Across this batch nobody attached playwright-cli, agent-browser, or browser-use to cuttle; nobody took a screenshot, hit a dialog, typed a secret, or handled a download. That absence is itself the signal: this batch cannot speak to page-level friction, and any conclusion about selectors, dialogs, or the VNC viewer would have to come from a different sample.

What this batch does show, consistently, is that cuttle's rough edges live at the operate-and-observe boundary, not the drive-a-page boundary. The same root cause appears four times in session 4 in different clothes: there is no machine-readable way to ask a running cuttle anything about itself. Is it ready to accept an attach? Which persona/arch is this seed serving? What address can *this* caller reach the CDP endpoint on? Is the port exposed to anything beyond loopback? Each unanswered question produced a workaround - a `sleep 12` plus curl poll, a hand-maintained manifest-digest pin with a warning comment, a `CUTTLE_SIDECAR_HOST` rewrite branch plus a legacy env fallback, and (only because an unrelated audit went looking) the discovery that an unauthenticated CDP surface was reachable by unauthenticated callers. A `cuttle status --json` that reported readiness, per-seed persona, reachable CDP address, and bind scope would have collapsed all four.

The second cross-cutting pattern is documentation shape deciding agent behavior. The human's correction - "it feels like you keep falling back to docker compose directly in most occasions" - drew an unusually clear admission: the agent picked the weaker tool because that tool's docs were written in agent-shaped commands (`up -d`, returns immediately, fits a tool-call loop) while the correct one was written for a human watching a dashboard. The cost was concrete: it skipped `cuttle-smoke`, the exact check for the browser path it was regression-testing. Anything cuttle wants agents to do must be documented as a non-interactive command sequence with a readiness gate and an exit code, or agents will route around it.

Third, timeouts are indistinguishable from impossibilities. The a cloud productivity app case burned two full 25-second renders on a page whose text was never in the DOM, and returned a bare `timeout` - the same outcome a slow-but-viable page would produce. A user hit that same wall three times, and their smartest attempt (rewriting to `/export?format=txt`) failed too because a browser "navigating" to a text/plain download produces nothing. cuttle currently gives callers no way to distinguish "needs more budget" from "this will never work in a browser", which is what turns one bad fetch into a retry loop.

One more thing worth noting for the wider audit: in two separate sessions the agent independently reached for glim MCP tools rather than a browser for every research task - and those tasks (reading GitHub repos, fetching articles, scraping HN via Firebase and Algolia) succeeded. Whatever draws agents toward cuttle, it is not general web reading; the demand is for logged-in, stateful, anti-bot surfaces. That should shape which frictions matter most.

**H9.** Five patterns are only visible with all four sessions side by side.

1. NOT ONE of the three real work sessions (S13, S14, S15) ever took a screenshot or opened the VNC viewer - and all three contain at least one episode where the agent stalled for 4-14 calls on something an image would have answered instantly (an invisible edit affordance, a dead search button, a disabled OAuth button, a "Show 4 more..." expander). The meta-session then independently found the same shape corpus-wide: 21/21 screenshots wasted in one session, yet screenshots were the *only* tool for cross-origin captchas and for catching a driver that lied "Done" three times. Agents have no calibration and no cuttle-native verb, so they default to never looking. This is the single highest-leverage missing capability across the batch.

2. Every session independently reinvented raw-JS DOM extraction as the escape hatch when the accessibility layer failed - and it failed constantly (missing Save button in S13, unusable a third-party site tree in S14, no ref for the OAuth link and a lazily-rendered list in S15). Two of those escapes ended in an injected `el.click()`, which bypasses cuttle's humanizer and emits isTrusted:false. So the recurring workaround for a UI-perception gap is the one move that destroys the product's core value. cuttle needs a trusted coordinate-click escape hatch and a documented structured-extract path, or agents will keep reaching for dispatchEvent.

3. The click-hang bug (fixed in session 4 as v0.11.4) is visibly present in the earlier sessions as *misdiagnosed* symptoms. S14's 5000ms Pesquisar timeout plus 15-iteration poll, and S13's ladder of clicks that changed nothing, both read at the time as site quirks. Nobody connected them until 2,100 calls were mined at once. The lesson beyond the fix: cuttle gives agents no way to distinguish "the daemon never received input" from "the site ignored the click", so a single root cause wore four disguises for two weeks.

4. Secrets are the most dangerous area and the least served. S14 produced an actual credential leak requiring rotation, plus an OTP pasted into the transcript in plaintext, plus a redactor that shredded the driver's own output when the secret was short. Session 4 then found the complementary hole: secrets cannot reach the run-code sandbox at all, so the efficient path and the safe path are mutually exclusive. The whole mechanism lives in the driver (PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_SECRETS_FILE), is all-or-nothing at attach time, and blocks reading legitimate page data. cuttle knows exactly what it typed and should own masking and injection at the CDP layer.

5. Three of four sessions show the agent sharing one browser with the human and reasoning about it by hand - S13 working around a freelance marketplace tabs, S15 attaching into a stale a consumer banking web app logout page and a logged-out a consumer banking app left by S14 the night before, and session 4 quantifying ~115 calls lost to tab-focus confusion and shared-browser contention plus the corpus's only user interrupt. There is no agent-owned tab concept, tab indices shift under batch closes, and `playwright-cli attach` lands on tab 0 (the human's). This is a missing cuttle primitive, not a driver quirk, and it is the second-most-repeated friction after not being able to see the page.

**H10.** Five of the six sessions drove cuttle (all on the same machine, cuttle 0.11.4, playwright-cli 0.1.17 as default driver); S24 used a standalone agent-browser in a different container with no cuttle involved.

Patterns only visible across the batch:

1. THE DOMINANT WORKAROUND IS "DON'T USE THE PAGE AT ALL". In 4 of 5 cuttle sessions the agent's real data path was `--raw eval` running `fetch()` against the site's private JSON API from page context (api.a freelance marketplace refresh-token -> Bearer -> /missions/open/v2, /user/v2/applied-missions; api.lu.ma/event/get-guest-list). It re-derived that auth flow from scratch each session. This is the loudest possible signal that reading a rendered page through cuttle is too expensive: the agent would rather reverse-engineer an undocumented API than snapshot a list. When the API shortcut failed (Luma 401), the fallback was shell forensics over snapshot YAML files.

2. THE PASSWORD STORY IS ONE STORY WITH A BAD ENDING. The identical `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(HTMLInputElement.prototype,'value').set` + `atob($(printenv ATEAM_PASSWORD | base64))` hack was independently invented in S23, S26 and S27 - three times, because no supported secret path exists. Then in S26 the agent ran the ordinary `snapshot` verb to debug a failed login and the a11y tree printed the password in cleartext: `textbox "Password..." [ref=e28]: t2J6bXyCQumuF!cukv`. The user's own docs already forbade this and the agent still did it, because snapshot is the only way to see why a login failed. A secret-injection verb plus snapshot-side redaction would close both halves at once.

3. LOGIN NEVER PERSISTS FOR THE ONE SITE THAT MATTERS. All three a freelance marketplace board checks (Aug 6, Aug 10, Aug 12) opened with an expired session (401 on refresh-token, or a redirect to /sign-in), despite the persistent profile, the 3008-cookie state snapshots visible in the daemon log, and the "logins live in this one and persist across down/up" promise in cuttle's own briefing. The agent has internalized the failure - "Logged out as expected (their sessions always expire)" - which means the cost is paid silently every session and drags the risky password flow along with it.

4. THE BROWSER IS A SHARED, DIRTY DESK. Every attach inherited the focused tab of whoever used the browser last: a professional social network developer-app pages, a SaaS console, a timed-out a consumer banking web app session, a a consumer banking app logged-out page, and `https://a media-index site/signup.php`. Twice the agent's first automatic snapshot was of a page belonging to an unrelated task. There is no notion of an agent-owned working tab.

5. THREE DIFFERENT WRONG TAB VERBS IN THREE SESSIONS - `new-tab`, `tabs`, `navigate` - each answered with a full usage dump and no "did you mean". The `cuttle up` briefing teaches attachment and driver routing but not a single page verb, and in S28 the agent truncated that briefing to `tail -2` to save context, discarding even the routing rules. Verb knowledge is the most reliably missing thing in the loop.

6. NOBODY EVER LOOKED AT THE SCREEN. Across five sessions and roughly 60 browser calls there is not one screenshot and not one mention of the VNC viewer on :6080, even in the two moments where seeing the page was the whole problem: a Sign In button that had three matching nodes and would not navigate, and a login that failed with nothing readable but `img "error"` and "Console: 10 errors". The substitute in both cases was `sleep 8` followed by polling `window.location.href`. The absence is not preference - there is no screenshot verb, so "what does this actually look like" is not a question the agent can ask.

7. NO FORM WAS EVER SUBMITTED BY AN AGENT. The two a freelance marketplace applications were pasted field-by-field by the human, who then dumped the entire rendered apply page (4,000+ words, including the country dropdown and a note that a cross-origin iframe could not be read) back into chat so the agent could verify what was submitted. With a warm logged-in browser attached and the exact form open, the loop still ended at "here is text for you to paste".

**H11.** Only 1 of the 6 sessions (S16) actually drove a browser; four were code reviews of cuttle or its consumer, and one was a meta-audit of two weeks of real browser sessions. That distribution is itself the finding: the friction is dense enough that most of the engineering attention in this batch went into diagnosing cuttle rather than using it.

Three patterns are only visible across sessions:

1. ONE root cause explains the corpus's single largest error class, and it took a dedicated forensic session to find. The meta-audit (session 3) counted 139 timeout results (4.9% of 2827 browser calls) and recorded a session that gave up on `playwright-cli click` entirely - "ALWAYS times out ... elements never report stable". The live session (session 5) proved it three days later: zero rAF in non-active tabs, plus playwright's noDefaults:true attach path skipping Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled. In between, every agent independently rediscovered the JS-eval workaround. The lesson is about diagnosability, not the bug: the error text named the element, cuttle's logs showed nothing (zero Input frames on the wire during the hang), and no verb existed to check tab foreground state. An agent CANNOT self-diagnose a failure whose cause is invisible from both ends.

2. SILENT STATE LOSS IS THE DOMINANT CLASS, and it arrives by four independent routes that all fail with err == nil: driver-created contexts whose cookies are never snapshotted (sessions 2, 6); the capture overwriting a good snapshot with a cookie-less one and then deleting the profile (session 6); idle-reap deleting the profile dir because --keep-profile defaults false (session 2); and hand-rolled deployments missing the volume + env var that the cuttle CLI sets, so the advertised "persistent logins" feature is silently absent (session 2). Session 3 adds the human-visible version: a cookie-replay experiment burned the live a professional social network login and forced the human to re-authenticate through VNC. Every one of these is invisible at the moment it happens. A single "your login will not survive because X" warning at the point of loss would collapse all four.

3. INVISIBLE-FAILURE-BY-DESIGN recurs at every layer, not just in state. Off-viewport clicks no-op with no error (session 3). A dead Chrome answers with a 502 whose body is valid JSON, so the client parses failure as success (session 4). A bare /json/version launches an unreapable browser (session 4). `playwright-cli attach` silently targets the human's tab (session 5). The window Chrome opens does not match either the spoofed screen or the 1920x1080 VNC framebuffer (session 1). The unifying gap: cuttle has no read-side. There is no `cuttle tabs`, no `cuttle screenshot`, no `cuttle probe` - so every attempt to answer "what is actually on screen / which tab am I on / is this page alive" was hand-rolled as raw CDP through a driver, and session 5 needed ten separate node scripts plus a wire trace to answer questions that should be one command.

Secondary: SKILL.md was read and followed and still produced wrong behaviour twice - it states "never create a new profile or context" absolutely while the drivers people actually use (playwright's newContext, scrapling's StealthyFetcher) mandate it, and it gives no concrete alternative (`browser.contexts()[0]`). And browser-use, one of the three advertised drivers, was used zero times in two weeks while playwright-cli took 72.7% of calls as "the unexamined default" - driver choice was template-copying, never deliberation.

**H12.** Five patterns only visible across the batch.

1. Nobody uses cuttle as a browser - they use it as a credentialed HTTP client. In four of the five cuttle sessions the entire task was `playwright-cli --raw eval` running `fetch()` against the site's private API from inside a logged-in page. The only real page-reading (a professional social network profile, session 2) cost seven shell calls of snapshot -> ls -t the YAML -> rg -> sed -> run-code scroll -> snapshot -> rg. The value cuttle actually delivers today is 'a tab that carries the user's cookies'; everything above that layer is the agent's own JS. cuttle has no verb for that use case and its briefing does not name it.

2. Zero screenshots and zero viewer use across all six sessions - including the one session (S31) that hit a hard login wall ('Privy needs an emailed code'), where the agent gave up on the real dashboard, verified against a throwaway stub route, shipped and released, and told the user 'One thing left for you, since I can't log in'. That session used `agent-browser --session-name surf` (its own browser), never `--cdp 9222`. The human-handoff viewer is cuttle's answer to exactly that wall and it never occurred to the agent, because the agent never loaded cuttle at all. The routing failure is upstream of any cuttle feature.

3. Tab contention with the live human is the single most repeated failure mode - three separate sessions, three different shapes: the agent's tab silently vanished (S32: 'my jup.ag tab is already gone'), the agent hijacked the human's tab and had to account for it in prose (S30: 'I left the cuttle browser's first tab on Amy's a professional social network profile'), and the human's tab caused a credentialed query to execute on the wrong origin (S34: a link-aggregator site stack trace surfacing as `jq: parse error`). Tab indices are positional and shared; there is no agent-owned target and no origin pinning.

4. Every session pays the same 2-4 call preamble - `which cuttle` / `cuttle --help` / `cuttle status` / `attach` / `tab-list` / `tab-new` - and two sessions only started it after the human explicitly said the word 'cuttle'. The briefing text is good but arrives too late and is re-read every time; nothing persists per-site knowledge, which is why the user in session 1 demanded a hand-written recipe in his own repo ('so that ... it would be the straightforward workflow for you without erroring on each step') and got a four-step playbook plus a baseline TSV committed to CLAUDE.md. That file is a user-authored substitute for a missing cuttle feature.

5. Secrets and session persistence are entangled. cuttle promises 'logins live in this one and persist across down/up', but the a freelance marketplace session lapsed within two days (S33), and the recovery path is a hand-rolled `Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(HTMLInputElement.prototype,'value').set` + `atob($(printenv PASS | base64))` injection with stdout suppressed, followed by a `click({force:true})` on a button the snapshot reports as `[disabled]`. Three separate missing capabilities (auth-freshness reporting, secret typing, post-injection form-state verification) all show up in one five-call sequence, and the user's own CLAUDE.md now encodes the hazard as a rule ('never in fill or run-code', 'don't snapshot between fill and submit'). No JS dialog, cookie banner, download or upload appeared in any of these six sessions - the frictions that actually fired were tab identity, origin drift, secret typing, session expiry, and page-text extraction.

**H13.** Six sessions, three of which actually drove cuttle (S36, S37 and S40 drove logged-in sites). Patterns visible only across them:

1) NOT ONE CDP INPUT EVENT WAS EVER SENT. Across all three cuttle sessions there is zero `click`, zero `type`, zero `fill`, zero `press`. Every interaction was a page load plus `eval`/`fetch`. cuttle's most expensive and most distinctive feature - humanized Input (curved mouse, log-normal keystrokes, typo injection, ~0.5s/click, ~130ms/char) - was exercised exactly never. In practice these agents used cuttle as "a logged-in cookie jar reachable over CDP", and two of three sessions bypassed the DOM entirely by calling the site's private API from inside the page. If that is representative, the cheapest wins are on the data-extraction and session-state axes, not the input-realism axis.

2) NOBODY EVER TOOK A SCREENSHOT - and two sessions hand-rolled a substitute. S36 built `location.href + body.innerText.slice(0,400)`; S40 read a driver-written `.yml` accessibility snapshot off disk. The recurring first question is always "where am I and what is showing", and there is no cuttle answer to it, so each session invents a different one. A single `cuttle peek` (url + title + text/aria digest, per tab) would have served all three.

3) THE LOGIN BOUNDARY IS THE ONLY PLACE HUMANS ARE STILL REQUIRED, AND IT IS UNINSTRUMENTED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. Before the handoff the agent cannot ask "is this origin still authenticated" (S36 wrote a site-specific `fetch('/api/retail/user/current/wallet')` probe); after it, the agent cannot detect completion and has to end its turn waiting for the human to type "its logged in". cuttle already stores per-seed auth snapshots - surfacing `auth status` and a blocking `wait-login` would close both halves and make unattended runs possible.

4) THE ATTACH/BRIEFING PATH IS GENUINELY GOOD - ZERO FAILURES. `cuttle up`/`status` printed the drivers, versions and exact attach commands, and every session attached correctly on the first try; there was not one failed cuttle or driver call in the entire batch (the only tool failures were Beeper 400s and a python typo). The friction is never "the command errored", it is always "the capability does not exist, so I wrote a workaround". Workarounds observed: innerText-slicing eval, private-API fetch replay, a bespoke `CUTTLE_CDP=... python` scraper (documented in the repo because "agent-browser's daemon socket cannot" carry a large return), giant shell-escaped JS piped through `jq -r .` into scratchpad files.

5) `cuttle up` IS NOT IDEMPOTENT-FEELING. The same command printed "already running", "running" and "restarted" across the three sessions; only the restart case silently discarded the tab set, and it gave no reason. Since this user habitually leaves logged-in tabs in the shared browser, a silent restart is real state loss with no notice.

6) TWO NON-DRIVING SESSIONS STILL SHOW CUTTLE COSTS WORTH KNOWING: in S39 the cuttle container measured 26-32% of a host core (roughly two-thirds of all OrbStack CPU) during a local performance investigation, and in S38 a local paid smoke reported bare 502/503 on the a link-aggregator site and a large e-commerce site endpoints purely because the cuttle sidecar was not running locally - no error anywhere said "cuttle unreachable", so the agent first read it as a payment regression. A clearer upstream-unavailable signal from the cuttle client would have saved that misattribution.

**H14.** Four of the six sessions in this batch never drove a browser at all - two Go code reviews of a cuttle diff, one cloakbrowser source analysis, one skills review - and a fifth was a meta-audit reading someone else's browser session. Only one session (S12) actually attached to cuttle. That ratio is itself a signal: the cuttle corpus is dominated by reasoning ABOUT cuttle rather than driving it, so friction evidence is concentrated in a handful of long sessions and is easy to miss.

Three independent threads in this batch converge on ONE root cause - focus/visibility of the target tab. (a) Session 5 proves cuttle's base Chrome args are a byte-identical copy of the deployed daemon's 8-flag list and omit Playwright's four backgrounding switches, so a background/occluded renderer never fires rAF and Playwright's 'stable' actionability check can never satisfy. (b) The audited session in #2 shows the agent-facing symptom twice: a 5000ms click timeout on a button the driver had already resolved, escalating through force-click and dispatchEvent (~10 calls, and the synthetic fallback silently did not persist). (c) The same audited session shows a second symptom of the same family - document.visibilityState 'hidden' broke IntersectionObserver lazy-loading and cost 8 calls of phantom debugging. The daemon's per-page Emulation.setFocusEmulationEnabled pin evidently does not cover either case. This is the single highest-leverage fix in the batch.

The window/viewport contract is broken from both ends and nobody owns it. Session 1 measured drivers overriding cuttle's careful sizing (Playwright viewport 1920x1080 vs a macOS seed whose screen is at most 1728 wide - inner > screen on 5/5 macOS seeds, 3/4 Windows seeds). Session 3 found the opposite failure in the daemon (a seed hashing to 2560x1440 launches --window-size=2560,1392 on a fixed 1920x1080 Xvfb, ~1.7x raster memory off-screen). Same seam, opposite directions, both invisible at runtime.

Every capability gap shows up as an invented workaround, and the workaround is the feature request: three throwaway TypeScript probe scripts written into the user's repo to answer 'did the page render' (missing screenshot/page-state verb); a shell for-loop doing scroll+capture (missing scroll-sweep); in-page fetch against a site's own API returning 5.5 KB in one call where clicking would have taken 50 (missing 'read behind a login' pattern); a driver-specific PLAYWRIGHT_MCP_SECRETS_FILE env var as the only safe way to type a password (missing cuttle-owned secret injection, and it silently does not apply if the agent picks the batched-JS verb).

Success strings lie in three different places and only vision catches it: `agent-browser upload` returned '✓ Done' three times on a no-op; `tab new` returned '✓ Screenshot saved' for an unreachable URL; a probe run reported '-> ok' on six navigations that all died on net::ERR_INVALID_AUTH_CREDENTIAL. In each case the disproof was a post-condition read or a screenshot. Any cuttle write verb should return the post-condition, not a checkmark.

The human's complaints are never about a single slow call - they are about browser-driving as a mode ('i hope it would be faster and less error-prone', 'every time i ask you to check a professional social network, do you need to run cuttle for that?'), and both times he proposed the correct alternative himself before the agent found it.

**H15.** STRUCTURAL NOTE FIRST: only 2 of the 4 assigned sessions have browser content, and neither drove a browser itself - both (S16 and S16) are friction-audit subagents spawned by the same parent in a project directory on 2026-08-08. This exact audit has already been run once. The primary sources are S06, S03, S24 and S05 - go to those for raw evidence, and do not double-count these two entries with any other analyst covering the same source ids.

PATTERN 1 - the single warm shared browser is the #1 failure mode, and it is invisible from both ends. Two independent sessions on two different days hit it: in S05 the human was browsing a SaaS console in the same Chrome she had just told the agent to use ('I'll do verification myself, meanwhile could you please use cuttle...'), and 13 straight coordinate clicks landed on her tab until she hit interrupt; in S06 a *second Claude session* stole the foreground and the human had to explain it ('there was a small interruption of another session using cuttle too'). `tab-select` reports `(current)` while `visibilityState` is `hidden`, so the driver's notion of 'current tab' and the browser's foreground are different things nothing surfaces. This is the highest-leverage fix in the whole batch: foreground/attached-client state in `cuttle status` and in every driver result, plus bringToFront guidance.

PATTERN 2 - silent success is worse than failure. Across all four source sessions the costly loops are the ones where an action returned a success-shaped result and changed nothing: CodeMirror inserts that exit 0 with the buffer unchanged, `el.click()` on a cloud provider's account chooser returning 'clicked account' with the URL unmoved, `click \"\"` on an empty ref no-opping, a bash ref-loop printing three empty arrows. Every one of these cost 10-20 calls because the agent had to invent its own verification eval. cuttle already has a settle gate and aria-toggle post-click verify - extending 'verify the mutation actually happened, else report failure' to fill/type would kill the largest single category of waste.

PATTERN 3 - blindness. S05 took ZERO screenshots in 183 browser calls and never once opened or mentioned the VNC viewer that `cuttle up` had printed for it, even when handing the task back to the human; S06 took 2 in 123. In both, one image at the moment of the third failed hypothesis would have ended a 13-call and a 9-call dead end. There is no `cuttle screenshot` verb, and nothing in the flow nudges an agent to look. 'When two consecutive actions produce no state change, look at the screen' is a one-line SKILL.md rule worth more than most features.

PATTERN 4 - inline JS in a shell argument is broken in every driver. playwright-cli run-code (21 of 40 calls written to a file), agent-browser eval (regex flags mangled by escaping, then 9 throwaway .js files), and S05's /tmp/inspect3.js, /tmp/usercap.js, /tmp/userhtml.js etc. Three different agents on two drivers independently invented the identical `cat > /tmp/x.js <<'EOF' ... EOF` workaround. Document the heredoc/--filename pattern as the default and the whole class disappears.

PATTERN 5 - no wait-for-condition, so agents sleep. 894s of sleep in S06 plus 7 hand-rolled polling loops (~40 min), 354s across 86 sleeps in S05, 602s of cold-start sleep in S03, and agent-browser padding `sleep 8` on top of its own `wait --load networkidle`. Notably nobody ever misread humanized input as a hang - the slowness complaints are about polling and cold start, not about the 0.5s click.
